| **DECIDED: LAYOUT-IN-CSS (requirement, recorded; partially honoured) — canvas/frame dimensions are BASIC CSS; JS must not adjust geometry on a WINDOW RESIZE** | 2026-09-16 | The user requirement: the canvas/frame dimensions are basic CSS (flex/grid over the container's own size) and **JS must not be involved in adjusting to a window resize at all**; JS may only persist OPERATOR-CHOSEN sizes (a gutter drag/double-click reset) as custom properties. Today `layout-state.ts` projects zone sizes AND a JS `--stage-weight` (`<size>fr`) into inline custom properties on `<html>` on every layout change — the stage track must become a plain CSS `minmax(0, 1fr)` with the operator sizes as the only JS-owned inputs, and no resize listener may exist. Companion requirement **ARCH-GNOSIS-OFFLOAD**: heavy document work (import/parse/index/query) belongs to the Gnosis engine (the `SOURCE-SWITCHABLE` `RagStore` seam anticipates `createRemoteRagStore`), so the shell's UI thread never locks up under document load. | user requirement 2026-09-16 (defects `CANVAS-DIMENSIONS-JS-DRIVEN` + `HEAVY-OPS-FREEZE-THE-PAGE` + `ARCH-GNOSIS-OFFLOAD`) |
| **DECIDED: ARCH-GNOSIS-OFFLOAD (proposal verdict) — the engine may own the STORE, never the render path; the snapshot seam is the only crossing; mitigations-first** | 2026-09-16 | The three-agent proposal gate returned **PROCEED-WITH-AMENDMENTS** (`docs/specs/gnosis-offload-review.md`): the original ask (Gnosis owns heavy document handling) is DEMOTED to a PARKED DESTINATION (O-6/O-7/O-8) with named external triggers, and the scheduled work becomes the shell-side liveness units: **O-0** (the staged measurement artifact — a HARD PRECONDITION, incl. the snapshot-pull stage), **O-5** (the long-task harness + pinned per-pass budget), **O-3** (drop the JS `--stage-weight`; stage = plain `minmax(0,1fr)`), **O-9** (pure-CSS collapse/minimize) and **O-10** (`LayoutState.panes[].order` as the slot authority — lands BEFORE O-1), then **O-1/O-2** (scoped `'content'` render; document open mounts only the stage payload, doc-nav bounded at CSS level with rows still GRAPH-RESIDENT). Shape = **Alt C**: the only crossing is `RagSnapshotPayload {nodes, edges, store, revision}` (main→renderer, async, PULL); the renderer never reads the store, the engine never sees an envelope; **`RagStore` reads stay SYNCHRONOUS** (no async interface); `revision` + a stale-drop rule close the change-notification gap; O-4 (traversal walk to a worker) is CONDITIONAL on O-0. Landing order O-0 → O-5 → O-9+O-3 → O-10 → O-1 → O-2, each unit a single-diff revert with its own red set. | proposal gate 2026-09-16 (`docs/specs/gnosis-offload-proposal.md`, `-review.md`) |
| **DECIDED: RAG-AUTHORITATIVE — the RAG layer is authoritative; the provident graph is a transient render materialization produced by a RAG traversal** | 2026-08-26 (marked **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21**) | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21 by `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`** (`docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.1 — the `GN-1` user ruling "Supersede now — engine owns document CRUD"; **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from the gate record §11.1 + §14.1 and the SUPERSEDED-table enumeration after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]**). The superseded clause is **"the RAG store is the persistent source of truth"** — **the authority moves engine-side**. The row is retained for provenance; its register/catalog provenance is preserved (the catalog's rows on this lineage are **cited, never edited**). The RAG store (RAG nodes + edges) is the persistent source of truth. The provident graph only exists after compilation by the RAG traversal (a `LegacyInitialData` envelope → `translateLegacy` → `renderProducingProcess`). The graph is a pure projection of the RAG store, re-traversed on ANY RAG-store change (content or structural — **CONTENT-EDIT-RE-TRAVERSAL** supersedes the earlier in-place state-slice content-edit path; the re-traversal is the renderer's response to the store change). Invariant: "the RAG store is always correct; the materialized graph is always re-derivable." The engine's graph-is-authoritative pin holds at the graph→HTML boundary. | user clarification 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §8.1/§9 |
| **DECIDED: SUBTREE-OWNERSHIP — a RAG object OWNS a subtree of Provident nodes (many-to-one back-reference `Map<ragNodeId, nodeId[]>`)** | 2026-08-26 | A RAG object maps to a subtree (a `contentNodes`-owned content root + its children), NOT a single node. The subtree root can link children and have no family parent, attached into a root-visible zone via placement/component logic. The RAG object's text = the markdown of its whole subtree, embedded as ONE chunk (a `ul` + its `li` children are one RAG object / one embedding). The back-reference is many-to-one, rebuilt per traversal. | user clarification 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §10 |
| **DECIDED: MARKDOWN-EXPORT-ONLY — markdown is an export-only surface; agent changes go through direct MCP updates** | 2026-08-26 | The `MarkdownAdapter` output is a read/export surface for the agent, NOT an edit surface (the ownership loss from D7 is accepted). Agent-driven changes go through direct MCP `edit`-group mutations to the RAG store, not markdown round-trips. Diffing markdown vs. generated source is a FUTURE SPECULATIVE feature (`docs/pending.md`). | user decision 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §11 |
| **DECIDED: FORM-CONTROL-EDITING — editing is commit-on-blur via a provident-rendered textarea/input, writing back to the source RAG object** | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-22 at `C9 U-EDIT-1`'s landing** by **`DECIDED: WHOLE-PAGE-EDITING`** (now IMPLEMENTED — see that row) + **`DECIDED: PAGE-COMMIT-CONTRACT`** + **`DECIDED: PAGE-STATE-OWNED-BY-THE-TAB`**: no per-node `textarea`/`input` editing control is authored or rendered any more (`src/main/traversal.ts` authors **no** `textarea` child; no `rag-textarea-*`/`rag-editor-*` defs; the rendered stage census is ZERO `<textarea>` in both modes — `tests/single-editable-surface.test.ts` `FS21` rows 643/652/661/675); editing is ONE page-local `contenteditable` surface. **SURVIVING CLAUSES (binding):** commit **on blur** as the commit trigger; the **dirty-edit guard** (a dirty page is never replaced by a rebuild); write-back to the store → **re-traversal** (never a zone-targeted state slice — `FS-10`/`placement-target-blocked`); the caret's host-side state keyed by the page subject (now the ACTIVE TAB id, not a RAG node id). Original date 2026-08-26 (retained as provenance) | Editing is a form control (textarea/input) committed on blur, writing back to the source RAG object (via the back-reference). NOT contenteditable (fights graph-is-authoritative; `DomAdapter.text` clobbers a live editor). NOT a zone-targeted state-slice (FS-10 blocks it — `placement-target-blocked`) — write back to the RAG store → re-traversal. A dirty-edit guard queues (not executes) a rebuild while a control is dirty. Caret/focus is host-side state keyed by RAG node id, restored after rebuild. | user confirmation 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §3b/§9 |
| **DECIDED: CONTENT-EDIT-RE-TRAVERSAL — a content edit writes back to the RAG store, then the renderer re-traverses in response to the store change** | 2026-08-26 | A content edit (MCP `edit.set_content` or UI commit-on-blur) writes back to the RAG store (via the back-reference), then triggers a re-traversal to re-materialize the graph consistently (including multi-parent duplicates). The `edit.set_content` tool updates the store; the re-traversal is the renderer's response to the store change. **Supersedes the "no re-traversal" note in Unit B §5.4 for `edit.set_content`** (the content op is still journaled as a `content` entry — Unit A §5.6; the re-traversal is the renderer's response, not a journaling concern). The renderer re-traverses on ANY RAG-store change (content or structural) — the strongest coherence guarantee. Re-traversal is the ONLY content-edit coherence mechanism (no in-place state-slice path). | `docs/specs/unit-d-editing.md` §4/§5.1.9 |
| **DECIDED: DERIVED-DOC-FLOW — doc-flow edges (doc-head/next-section/doc-end) are authoritative in the RAG store; the traversal maps them to family order + a doc-head marker prop** | 2026-08-26 | The RAG store carries doc-flow edges as authoritative. The traversal realizes them in the engine as family child-anchor order + a doc-head marker prop (no new engine roles — closed `Role` union). The traversal VALIDATES the edges (cycle/missing-node/missing-head/missing-end) and falls back to family pre-order on violation. | user clarification 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §3c/§9.2.3 |
| **DECIDED: LEXICAL-FIRST-RETRIEVAL — BM25/tf-idf behind an interface-swappable `Embedder`; graph traversal for context assembly** | 2026-08-26 (implemented 2026-08-27, Units E + F) | Retrieval is lexical-first (deterministic, offline, testable) behind an `Embedder` interface; vector embeddings implemented (Unit F, 2026-08-27). Selection scores all RAG nodes, takes top-k, then assembles context by graph traversal (bounded). The line→node map is a first-class assembly output (coarse: whole subtree → one RAG object). **Implemented (Unit E):** `src/main/retrieval.ts` — the `Embedder` interface + the lexical (BM25) v1 default, the maintained lexical index (incremental on `onStoreChanged`), `selectTopK`/`assembleContext`/`retrieve`/`createRetrieval`, and MCP/UI equivalence (the `rag.query` MCP tool and the `rag-query` IPC call the same maintained engine, `main.ts`). **Implemented (Unit F):** the `Embedder` interface is amended to ASYNC and the vector embedder (`src/main/embeddings.ts`) is a drop-in behind the SAME seam — `retrieval.embedder: 'lexical' | 'vector'` selects it in `main.ts`; the engine uses the passed embedder unchanged. | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §3d/§9.2.10, `docs/specs/unit-e-rag-index.md` §4/§5, `docs/specs/unit-f-embeddings.md` §4/§5 |
| **DECIDED: SINGLE-WRITER-STORE — the RAG store is the lock point; the main process owns all writes; MCP and UI both route through it** | 2026-08-26 (marked **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21**) | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21 by `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`** (`docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.1 — the `GN-1` user ruling; **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from the gate record §11.1 + §14.1 and the SUPERSEDED-table enumeration after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]**). The superseded clause is **"the main process owns all writes; MCP and UI both route through it"** — **the lock point moves** (the commit path becomes an async engine write; see the write-path reversal clause on `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`). The row is retained for provenance. **Still owed by others:** `U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH` (O-8) lands the offline dual-path / offline-write reconciliation, and the per-store mapping of the lock point is that unit's work; the O-8 pointer row in `docs/HANDOFF.md` records the engine-side half. A main-process `createJsonRagStore` (module-store pattern) serializes writes through a queue/mutex. MCP `edit` tool handlers and the renderer's commit-on-blur IPC both call the same main-process store methods. No renderer-side writes to the RAG store. This is the concurrency model for MCP↔UI equivalence. | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §9.2.6 |
| **DECIDED: RAG-EDIT-MCP-GROUPS — new read-only `rag` + mutating `edit` MCP tool groups, both default-off, through the five-seam gate** | 2026-08-26 | `rag` (read-only, default-off): `rag.query`, `rag.get_document`, `rag.list_nodes`, `rag.get_edges`, `rag.backlinks`. `edit` (mutating, default-off): `edit.set_content`, `edit.create_node`, `edit.delete_node`, `edit.split_node`, `edit.merge_node`, `edit.set_edge`. Editing is NEVER a `code`-group op. Registered through the five-seam gate (`security.ts` TOOL_GROUPS + `mcp-server.ts` ALL_TOOLS + `shared/types.ts` RpcMethod + renderer switch + MUTATING_METHODS). | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §9.2.7 |
| **DECIDED: MULTI-PARENT-DUPLICATE — a multi-parent RAG node is materialized as duplicate subtrees (distinct content roots sharing the RAG id via the map), respecting SI-1** | 2026-08-26 | The engine's single-parent family model (SI-1) cannot represent a multi-parent RAG node. Duplicate-per-parent (each a distinct provident content root sharing the same RAG object id via the back-reference map) is the only option that respects the engine model. Content edits must update ALL materialized duplicates (state-slice every duplicate), or explicitly accept cross-duplicate staleness until the next structural rebuild. | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §9.2.8 |
| **DECIDED: PROJECT-JOURNAL — undo/redo lives in the RAG store's own project journal, not the engine Supervisor journal** | 2026-08-26 | The engine Supervisor journal dies on rebuild (a re-traversal mints a fresh graph). Undo/redo lives in the RAG store's project journal, which records INVERTIBLE entries for both content and structural ops (undoing a structural edit re-traverses). | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §9.2.1 |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-GAP-HANDOFF — a missing capability is an engine gap (handoff) iff it cannot be implemented by composing existing engine primitives in host code** | 2026-08-26 | Transient graph, back-reference, and RAG→subtree traversal are all project-specific (compose `translateLegacy`/`loadEnvelope`/`renderProducingProcess`/managed channel/five-seam gate). The ONE engine gap: `MarkdownAdapter` `data-node-id` preservation (D7) → handoff doc, non-blocking (the host-side line→node map covers it). The RAG layer is project-specific, not a foundation feature. | user note 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §8.3/§9.2.11 |
| **DECIDED: DOC-CHILD — a RAG object can own a subtree that CONTAINS nested subtrees owned by doc-child RAG objects (nested semantic units)** | 2026-08-26 | A nested provident node that is large enough for semantic distinctiveness (e.g. a paragraph-length `li` inside a `ul`) is its own RAG object, a doc-child of the containing RAG object. The `doc-child` edge (`{ kind: 'doc-child', source, target, order }` — `source` = the containing RAG object, `target` = the nested RAG object, `order` = the child's subtree position) expresses HIERARCHICAL nesting, distinct from the linear doc-flow edges (`doc-head`/`next-section`/`doc-end`). A RAG object's `ownedNodeIds` EXCLUDES the nodes owned by its doc-children. The traversal materializes doc-child subtrees at their `order` position within the parent's subtree. A doc-child's text = the markdown of its OWN subtree, embedded as a SEPARATE chunk. | user clarification 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §12 |
| **DECIDED: SOURCE-SWITCHABLE — the `RagStore` interface is the abstraction layer between the JSON store and the document load** | 2026-08-26 | `RagStore` (§5.4) is the INTERFACE the document load (Unit C traversal) and the MCP `rag`/`edit` handlers depend on — NOT the concrete JSON store. `createJsonRagStore` is the JSON-file implementation; a future `createRemoteRagStore` (pending — `docs/pending.md`) implements the SAME interface. The store is injected (constructor/options), never imported directly by the traversal/handlers, so the source is switchable without changing the document load. | user request 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/unit-a-rag-store.md` §5.3 |
| **DECIDED: STRUCTURAL-ROOT — a RAG object's subtree root can be a STRUCTURAL element type (e.g. `div`), not just a semantic element** | 2026-08-26 | A `div` (and other structural element types) is a valid `RagNodeType` root. It groups semantically-related but syntactically-unstructured text lines into ONE RAG object / one semantic chunk (e.g. a lazy/shorthand note like "S3 Bucket types / General Purpose / Vector / Directory / Table" written as plain lines with no `ul`/`ol` structure). The `div` root's children are the text lines; the whole subtree is one RAG object / one embedding. Same subtree-ownership model as `ul`/`ol` — the structural root is a grouping container. | user clarification 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §14 |
| **DECIDED: CROSS-DOCUMENT-SHARED — a RAG node can appear in MULTIPLE documents at the same time (shared/transcluded nodes)** | 2026-08-26 | A shared node (e.g. A's spec, called by both Class B and Class C) has MULTIPLE `parent-child` edges (one per document) and is materialized as duplicate subtrees in each document, all sharing the RAG id via the back-reference map. A text change updates all duplicates ("if the text of A changes, so do both documents"). **An edge carries a `documentIds: string[]` field (the document root node ids that OWN/USE the edge) — an edge can have MULTIPLE document owners** (a shared reference/content edge, e.g. the A→D edge, lists both B and C). A `next-section` edge's TARGET differs per document, so those are separate edges each with ONE owner. The traversal assembles each document's flow separately. Cost: a node referenced by N documents is materialized as N duplicate subtrees (the price of respecting SI-1). **Material-state nuance (2026-08-26):** the N duplicates are PER-RENDER materializations; render is transient. In the single-document view only ONE document renders at a time, so only ONE instance of a shared subtree exists at any moment (documents cannot loop in one view). The N duplicates COEXIST only if multiple documents render simultaneously — which requires UI TABS (a pending feature, `docs/pending.md`). | user clarity check 2026-08-26, `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §13 |
| **DECIDED: PROVIDER-AGNOSTIC — all agent/model-specific tasks (embeddings, and any future model calls) are provider/model AGNOSTIC, including remote/cloud-sourced providers, with config settings for the required inputs (model URL, API keys, etc.)** | 2026-08-27 (implemented 2026-08-27, Unit F) | The implementation of any model/agent-specific capability is NOT hardcoded to one provider/model. It is provider/model agnostic behind an interface, configurable to point at ANY provider — local (ollama `embeddinggemma`) OR remote/cloud — via config settings for the required inputs (model URL, API keys, model name, etc.). The `Embedder` interface (Unit E) is the seam; the vector embedder (Unit F) is a configurable provider, with ollama `embeddinggemma` as ONE concrete config and remote/cloud providers as others. **Implemented (Unit F, 2026-08-27):** `src/main/embeddings.ts` — the `EmbeddingProvider` abstraction + `EmbeddingProviderConfig` config shape, the ollama `embeddinggemma` concrete provider (localhost), the remote/cloud provider drop-in (OpenAI/Cohere/etc. via the SAME interface + config), and the `connect-src` CSP allowlist + API-key handling as a DESIGNED security surface. **Security implication:** supporting remote/cloud providers means network egress IS in scope — the pending.md `connect-src` CSP allowlist + API-key handling become a designed security surface (not just an open tracked item). | user clarification 2026-08-27, `docs/specs/unit-f-embeddings.md` |
| **DECIDED: CROSSLINK-EDGE-KIND — a `crosslink` RAG edge kind (`{ kind: 'crosslink', source, target, documentIds? }`) is the authoritative crosslink representation** | 2026-08-27 | A crosslink is a NEW RAG edge kind `crosslink` in the `RagEdgeKind` union — the authoritative representation. It reuses the `RagEdge` shape (Unit A §5.1), the store's edge CRUD, and the project journal. It is NOT a node property and NOT a separate link registry. The RAG store is authoritative; the traversal materializes the graph; the backlink enumeration reads the store. | `docs/specs/unit-g-crosslink-backlink.md` §4 |
| **DECIDED: CROSSLINK-LINKCONFIG — a custom `LinkConfig` with `name: 'crosslink'`, `roles: ['source', 'target']` materializes crosslinks into the provident graph** | 2026-08-27 | The custom crosslink `LinkConfig` is `{ name: 'crosslink', roles: ['source', 'target'] }` — a custom name in the engine's OPEN `LinkConfig.name` union. The traversal materializes each outgoing crosslink edge as a DISTINCT `Link` (created directly via `new Link(CROSSLINK_LINK_CONFIG)`, NOT via `createLinkHub` — the hub is the engine's same-name sharing mechanism for `component`/`placement` links and would inherit parent-child roles for a custom name). The `Link` carries a `source` anchor on the source RAG node's subtree root and a `target` anchor on the target RAG node's subtree root. | `docs/specs/unit-g-crosslink-backlink.md` §4 |
| **DECIDED: PANE-REGISTRY — a host-side `PaneRegistry` is the single authority over which sidebar panes exist and which are enabled** | 2026-08-28 | `PaneDefinition` = `{ id, title, scope: 'app-graph' | 'operator', render }`. The registry provides the 9 methods `register`/`get`/`list`/`listByScope`/`isEnabled`/`enable`/`disable`/`setEnabled`/`onChanged`. Newly registered panes are DISABLED; the host enables the panes it wants (the app-graph panes must be enabled to be MCP-visible). A PURE module (no Electron) — testable in isolation. Implemented in `src/renderer/pane-registry.ts`. | `docs/specs/unit-h-sidebar-panes.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: PANE-PROVIDENT-AUTHORING — every pane's content is authored as provident-ssr data and rendered through the producing graph** | 2026-08-28 | Each pane's `render(ctx)` returns a `LegacyNodeData` subtree; app-graph panes are authored into the pane-inclusive app-graph envelope (via `paneSubtreeRoot`, `src/renderer/pane-graph.ts`), operator panes into the isolated-scope envelope (`buildOperatorEnvelope`). A pane rendered outside the provident graph is a review finding (the project-wide constraint, AGENTS.md). | `docs/specs/unit-h-sidebar-panes.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: APP-GRAPH-PANES-MCP-VISIBLE — the document-navigation, crosslink/backlink, and search panes render in the app Runtime graph → MCP-visible** | 2026-08-28 | The `doc-nav`/`crosslinks`/`search` panes are `scope: 'app-graph'`. Their `render(ctx)` subtrees are authored into the pane-inclusive envelope the app Runtime renders, so `get_rendered_html`/`get_markdown`/`list_targets`/`get_node_state`/`provident.dispatch` see them (the §8.2 BINDING equivalence). | `docs/specs/unit-h-sidebar-panes.md` §4/§5.3/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: OPERATOR-ISOLATED-GRAPHSCOPE — the settings pane is `scope: 'operator'` and renders in an isolated `createIsolatedScope()` GraphScope, never MCP-visible** | 2026-08-28 | The settings pane renders in its OWN `createIsolatedScope()` `GraphScope` (own `Supervisor` + own `DomAdapter` → own mount, mirroring `SecurePanels`), disjoint from the app graph. It is NOT MCP-visible and NOT exposed to the `rag`/`edit` groups — an agent cannot read it, list it as a target, dispatch on it, or mutate it. Its edits are operator edits through the IPC bridge, never the RAG `edit.*` path. | `docs/specs/unit-h-sidebar-panes.md` §4/§5.4/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: CONTENT-WINDOW-TEMPLATE — the content-window template IS the `LegacyInitialData.template` shape; the default is the current FIXED `wiki-root` + per-zone containers** | 2026-08-28 | The content-window template is the `LegacyInitialData.template` shape (`{ root: LegacyNodeData }`), the field that defines the content-window layout (the root structure + the container zones) for rendering a retrieved document. The default (`DEFAULT_CONTENT_WINDOW_TEMPLATE`) is the current FIXED `wiki-root` + one `main` zone container (the Unit C baseline, `src/main/traversal.ts`). A customized template replaces it. | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: TEMPLATE-STORE — a host-side main-process template store persists the current `ContentWindowTemplate`, separate from the RAG store** | 2026-08-28 | A host-side main-process `createTemplateStore` persists the current `ContentWindowTemplate` (a JSON file, `node:fs` atomic temp+rename, fail-disabled boot to the default). It is SEPARATE from the RAG store — the template is NOT RAG content (it is the envelope's `template`, not a document-graph node/edge). | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: CODE-GROUP-TEMPLATE-CRUD — the template CRUD is exposed as the `code.template.*` tools in the `code` group, main-handled, default-off** | 2026-08-28 | The template CRUD is exposed as the `code.template.*` tools (`get`/`validate`/`set`/`create`/`delete`/`reset`), ALL in the `code` group (default-off), main-handled against the template store through the five-seam gate (the Unit B §5.3 pattern). Editing the template is a `code`-group op — consistent with the gate line 60 ("`code.*` CRUD") and the existing `provident.code.*` envelope-editing group. | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: TEMPLATE-PANE — a provident-rendered `scope: 'app-graph'` template-editor pane, Unit H authoring** | 2026-08-28 | A provident-rendered `scope: 'app-graph'` template-editor pane (`id: 'template-editor'`) follows the Unit H authoring convention. It renders the current template structure (the root + the zones) as editable provident content and commits edits via the template IPC (the SAME template store the `code.template.*` MCP tools reach). Being app-graph, it is MCP-visible: an agent reads it via `get_rendered_html`/`get_markdown` and dispatches its handlers via `provident.dispatch`, OR drives the `code.template.*` tools directly (§8.2 equivalence). | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.4 |
| **DECIDED: ZONE-CONSISTENCY-INVARIANT — a customized template MUST NOT drop a `container`-role producer for a zone the traversal targets** | 2026-08-28 | A customized template MUST NOT drop a `container`-role producer for a zone the traversal targets (the Unit C HARD PRECONDITION — a missing container leaves the content `unplaced`, silently not render-eligible). Enforced at TWO layers: (a) save-time validation (`validateTemplate` + the store's `set`/`create`/`delete`) REJECTS a template missing a targeted zone and forbids deleting a targeted zone; (b) the traversal (`buildTraversal`) defense-in-depth ADDS a missing `zoneName` producer (never leaves the subtree unplaced). | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: TEMPLATE-RE-DERIVE — a template change triggers a whole-graph re-derive, reusing the Unit C traversal + Unit H assembly** | 2026-08-28 | A template change (via the MCP `code.template.*` tools OR the UI template IPC) writes the template store, then main broadcasts `template-changed` → the renderer re-derives the whole graph: `buildTraversal` (with the stored template) → `assembleAppGraphEnvelope` → the app Runtime re-render. Infrequent, so the whole-graph cost is acceptable (review §4 item 9). | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §4/§5.5 |
| **DECIDED: MCP-UI-EQUIVALENCE — every MCP tool with a UI IPC counterpart routes through the SAME shared handler (the §8.2 BINDING constraint)** | 2026-08-26 (pinned by Unit J 2026-08-28) | The same graph, the same rendering, and the same operations are reachable equivalently through the MCP surface and the Electron UI. Every MCP tool with a UI IPC counterpart routes through the SAME shared handler: `rag.query`/`rag-query` (the same maintained retrieval engine), `rag.backlinks`/`rag-backlinks` (the same `enumerateLinks`), `edit.set_content`/`edit-commit` (the same `setContent` op), `code.template.*`/`IPC_TEMPLATE_*` (the same `handleTemplateTool`). The equivalence surface is a first-class, equivalent access path, not an afterthought. | `docs/specs/astrographer-review.md` §8.2, `docs/specs/unit-j-mcp-security-hardening.md` §4/§5.4 |
| **DECIDED: HARDENING-VERIFICATION-CONTRACT — the Unit J hardening pass is a VERIFICATION contract over the already-implemented `rag`/`edit`/`code.template.*` surfaces** | 2026-08-28 | The hardening pass does NOT add new behavior — it audits the implemented surfaces and pins the invariants (a)–(f) in `docs/specs/unit-j-mcp-security-hardening.md` §5.2 that MUST hold: (a) every `rag.*` tool is read-only + `rag`-group + default-off; (b) every `edit.*` tool is mutating + `edit`-group + default-off; (c) editing is NEVER a `code`-group op; (d) every MCP tool with a UI IPC counterpart routes through the SAME handler; (e) the renderer switch fails closed on unknown methods; (f) `MUTATING_METHODS` covers every mutating method. A TestWriter derives every state and fail-state from §5.8/§5.9. If a future change breaks an invariant, it is a review finding. | `docs/specs/unit-j-mcp-security-hardening.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: IPC-SURFACE-NOT-GROUP-GATED — the renderer→main IPC channels are NOT group-gated; the `rag`/`edit`/`code` groups gate the MCP agent path only** | 2026-08-28 | The renderer→main IPC channels (`edit-commit`, `rag-query`, `rag-backlinks`, `IPC_TEMPLATE_*`) are NOT group-gated — the renderer is a trusted surface that calls main directly (`contextIsolation:true`, `nodeIntegration:false`). The `rag`/`edit`/`code` groups gate the MCP AGENT path only. This is a documented design decision, not a gap. | `docs/specs/unit-j-mcp-security-hardening.md` §4/§5.7 |
| **DECIDED: EDIT-COMMIT-RETURN-ASYMMETRY — the `edit.set_content` MCP tool returns `SetContentResult` (the full updated node) while the `edit-commit` IPC returns `EditCommitResult` (the nodeId only)** | 2026-08-28 | The `edit.set_content` MCP tool returns `SetContentResult` (the full updated node), while the `edit-commit` IPC returns `EditCommitResult` (the nodeId only). BOTH call the same `setContent` op and produce the same store state + re-traversal; the return shape differs because the IPC is the renderer's commit contract. This is a documented asymmetry, not a gap. | `docs/specs/unit-j-mcp-security-hardening.md` §4/§5.7 |
| **DECIDED: UI-MOUNT-BOOT — the renderer bootstraps the app Runtime with the pane-inclusive envelope derived from the RAG store + the stored template, replacing the `demoEnvelope()` bootstrap** | 2026-08-28 | The `SidebarPanes` host's `boot(runtime)` replaces the `demoEnvelope()` placeholder bootstrap: it fetches the RAG snapshot + the stored template, derives the document ids, runs `buildTraversal` (with the stored template), assembles the pane-inclusive envelope, and loads it into the app Runtime — so the RAG content + the app-graph panes are MCP-visible by construction. The host owns the current-document/node state (M5). | `docs/specs/unit-k-sidebar-panes-host.md` §5.1/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: UI-MOUNT-RE-DERIVE — both `rag-store-changed` and `template-changed` trigger the SAME re-derive, guarded by the dirty-edit guard** | 2026-08-28 | A `rag-store-changed` or `template-changed` broadcast routes through the edit controller's dirty-edit guard (`requestRebuild`) → the host's `reDerive()`: fetch snapshot → `buildTraversal` (with the stored template) → `assembleAppGraphEnvelope` → re-load the pane-inclusive envelope + repopulate the backRefs map. The edit controller's `onRebuild` IS the host's `reDerive` (the SOLE subscription; the renderer-level duplicate is removed). Re-derive coalesces in-flight requests. | `docs/specs/unit-k-sidebar-panes-host.md` §5.2 |
| **DECIDED: UI-MOUNT-PANE-REGISTRATION — the four app-graph panes + the operator settings pane are registered + enabled by the host, and their handlers are bound to the IPC bridge** | 2026-08-28 | The host's `registerPanes()` registers the four app-graph panes (`doc-nav`/`crosslinks`/`search`/`template-editor`) + the operator `settings` pane and enables them; `bindHandlers()` registers the handler defs via `registerHandlerDef` (the engine resolves the bodies by name). The host installs the `window.provident.sidebar` bridge surface (M2) the compiled handler bodies call. | `docs/specs/unit-k-sidebar-panes-host.md` §5.3 |
| **DECIDED: UI-MOUNT-OPERATOR — the settings pane mounts in an isolated `createIsolatedScope()` GraphScope via `mountOperator`, never MCP-visible** | 2026-08-28 | The operator settings pane renders in its OWN isolated provident GraphScope (`createIsolatedScope()`) in the `#operator-panes` mount (NOT `#panes`, which stays SecurePanels'; M3). Because it is a separate scope from the app Runtime, the MCP endpoints (which read the app Runtime) can never see or dispatch it. The operator-settings IPC (`operatorSettings.get`/`set`) is the M9 surface; the `topK` setting feeds the search pane's `bridge.rag.query`. | `docs/specs/unit-k-sidebar-panes-host.md` §5.4 |
| **DECIDED: RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE — the rich-text contenteditable editing proposal passed the three-agent gate as PROCEED-WITH-AMENDMENTS, sequenced textarea-first** | 2026-08-28 (**IN-HOUSE-BUILD CLAUSE SUPERSEDED 2026-09-22 at `C9 U-EDIT-1`'s landing** — the decomposer is the ADOPTED `provident-editable@0.2.0` (`htmlToTree`/`diffTrees`) with `src/main/page-diff.ts` as the pinned adapter; the in-house `src/main/rich-decompose.ts` premise and the `provident-editable@0.1.0` import plan are BOTH retired as the decomposer's provenance. **ALL OTHER CLAUSES OF THIS ROW SURVIVE AND STAY BINDING:** the `setProps` **MERGE** semantics preserving `data-doc-head`; **`setType` never delete+create**; the `children` inline model (`span` not added to `RagNodeType`; inline `strong`/`em`/`a`/`img` as `RagNodeChild[]`); the census rows (the edit-op census is 11 today — `DECIDED: EDIT-OP-CENSUS`); and the sequenced-slice record (textarea-first, then the rich-text machinery, now both landed). The rule was recorded in advance at `DECIDED: RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE-IN-HOUSE-CLAUSE-SUPERSEDED`, whose owed half is **DISCHARGED by this marker** — see the SUPERSEDED table below.) | The rich-text converter is built IN-HOUSE as `src/main/rich-decompose.ts` (Unit U2, the pure `decomposeRichHtml` module — the `provident-editable@0.1.0` import plan was replaced by the in-house build), and the slice is SEQUENCED: land the plain-text textarea editing UI (Unit D §5.6, deferred per Unit D §3a H5) FIRST, then the rich-text contenteditable machinery. Resolved design: (1) `span` is NOT added to `RagNodeType` — it is a diff-matching artifact folded into the parent's `content`; inline `strong`/`em`/`a`/`img` are held by a NEW `children` field on `RagNode` (not separate RAG nodes — preserves one-chunk-per-subtree); (2) `setProps` MERGES (preserves the `data-doc-head` marker), plus new `setSubtree` (updates `children`) and `setType` (type changes, never delete+create) ops; (3) plain-text is the DEFAULT for all nodes in v1; rich-text is opt-in per-node-type (leaf-no-inline-children → rich-text; rich subtrees → textarea); no global `editingMode` field. Must-fix before the rich-text spec: batch atomicity (a real transaction, not `store.enqueue`), retrieval indexing of inline `children` text, traversal disambiguation of inline vs doc-children, store-format `children` additive + hash-source, paste-time sanitization, census 6→9. **Batch atomicity MET 2026-08-28 (Unit N)** — the `applyBatch`
  transaction primitive (a real transaction, not `store.enqueue`) has landed on
  the `RagStore` interface (see BATCH-ATOMICITY-API). **Textarea-first
  prerequisite MET 2026-08-28 (Unit L)** — the plain-text textarea editing UI has landed. **Store-format `children` additive + hash-source MET 2026-08-28 (Unit M)** — the `children` field on `RagNode` + the `RagNodeChild`/`RagNodeChildType` types + the `nodeSource` hash coverage + the additive load + the write-time validation have landed (see CHILDREN-ADDITIVE-STORE-FORMAT / CHILDREN-HASH-SOURCE). **Census 6→9 MET 2026-08-28 (Unit O)** — the edit-op count goes from 6 to 9 (the three rich-text ops `setProps`/`setSubtree`/`setType` land in `src/main/edit-ops.ts`; see RICH-TEXT-EDIT-OPS). **Retrieval indexing of inline `children` text MET 2026-08-28 (Unit Q)** — the retrieval module indexes + renders the inline `children` text via the new `nodeText(node)` helper (see NODETEXT-SPACE-JOIN / RENDER-DIRECT-CONCAT). **Traversal disambiguation of inline vs doc-children MET 2026-08-28 (Unit R)** — the traversal renders the inline `children` as child elements of the subtree root, disambiguated from doc-children by the `rag-` id prefix (see INLINE-CHILDREN-AUTHORED-ID). **Paste-time sanitization MET 2026-08-28 (Unit S)** — the pure `sanitizePastedHtml` module normalizes pasted HTML into the `RagNodeChild[]` shape (see PASTE-SANITIZATION). **All RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE must-fix items are now MET (2026-08-28, Units N/O/P/Q/R/S).** | proposal gate 2026-08-28 (validity VALID-WITH-AMENDMENTS, critique UNSOUND-as-written, architecture SOUND-WITH-AMENDMENTS, change-analysis PROCEED-WITH-AMENDMENTS) |
| **DECIDED: TEXTAREA-PROVIDENT-AUTHORING — the textarea is authored as provident-ssr data in the traversal (a `textarea` child of each RAG subtree root), NOT hand-written HTML/DOM** | 2026-08-28 | The textarea is authored as provident-ssr data in `buildSubtree` (`src/main/traversal.ts`) — a `textarea` child of each RAG subtree root, bound to the RAG node's content via the back-reference map. NOT hand-written HTML/DOM. A textarea rendered outside the provident graph is a review finding (the project-wide all-UI-via-provident constraint, A1). | `docs/specs/unit-l-textarea-editing-ui.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: TEXTAREA-BRIDGE-SURFACE — the textarea's `onInput`/`onBlur` handlers reach the edit controller through the `window.provident.sidebar` bridge surface** | 2026-08-28 | The textarea's `onInput`/`onBlur` handlers reach the edit controller through the `window.provident.sidebar` bridge surface (the Unit K §5.3 M2 pattern), extended with `textareaInput`/`textareaBlur`. A compiled function-string handler body cannot call `markDirty`/`commit` directly (they are host methods, not reachable from a `new Function` body). The handlers NEVER call an MCP tool. | `docs/specs/unit-l-textarea-editing-ui.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: TEXTAREA-READONLY-HOST-SET — the textarea's `readOnly` prop is set by the host at render time from `editController.isEditable(ragId)`** | 2026-08-28 | The `readOnly` prop is set by the host at render time from `editController.isEditable(ragId)` (the traversal is pure and cannot see the edit controller). The traversal emits NO `readOnly` prop (editable by default — emitting `readOnly: false` would render as the `readonly` boolean attribute and make the textarea uneditable, adversarial H1); the host sets `readOnly: true` only when `!isEditable(ragId)` and omits it otherwise (idempotent across re-assembles, H4). | `docs/specs/unit-l-textarea-editing-ui.md` §4/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: NAME-REFERENCED-HANDLER-RESOLUTION — the app Runtime resolves name-referenced handler bodies (registered via `registerHandlerDef`) onto the node so the engine's `dispatchEvent` can fire them** | 2026-08-28 | The engine's `translateLegacy` only compiles INLINE handler bodies; a name-referenced handler (`{ name, event }`, body registered via `registerHandlerDef`) keeps `body` undefined and is skipped by dispatch. The app Runtime's `loadEnvelope`/`loadDoc` now resolves each name-referenced handler's body from the registry onto the node (`resolveNameReferencedHandlerBodies`), so the engine's `dispatchEvent` can fire it. This is the Conflict B resolution — it also fixes the pre-existing template-handler dispatch gap. The textarea's handlers are name-referenced, so this makes the textarea MCP-dispatchable (MCP/UI equivalence). | `docs/specs/unit-l-textarea-editing-ui.md` §5.8 item 12 |
| **DECIDED: TEXTAREA-RENDER-ONLY-OVERLAY — the textarea is a RENDER-ONLY editing overlay present in the DOM render view, NOT in the markdown** | 2026-08-28 | The subtree root's `content` is KEPT in the envelope (Conflict C resolution) so the markdown/line→node map (HOST-C1) still renders the text; the textarea is a RENDER-ONLY editing overlay present in the DOM render view, NOT in the markdown (the `MarkdownAdapter` does not render a `textarea`). The textarea's `value` mirrors the root's `content`. The textarea's authored id is `textarea-<ragId>` (NOT `rag-`-prefixed — `collectSubtreeIds` treats a `rag-`-prefixed child as a doc-child subtree root and would exclude the textarea from the backRefs map). | `docs/specs/unit-l-textarea-editing-ui.md` §5.1 |
| **DECIDED: CHILDREN-ADDITIVE-STORE-FORMAT — the `children` field on `RagNode` is an ADDITIVE, backward-compatible store-format change** | 2026-08-28 | The `children?: RagNodeChild[]` field on `RagNode` (the closed `RagNodeChildType` union `strong`/`em`/`a`/`img`; `span` NOT a member and NOT added to `RagNodeType`) is OPTIONAL and ADDITIVE — existing records without `children` still load and hash-verify (a missing `children` serializes identically to `children: undefined`), no migration/re-hash. `children` is deep-copied on BOTH write and read, validated at write (throw) and skipped at boot, and NOT part of `ownedNodeIds` (the inline children are held on the owning node, not separate subtree roots). | `docs/specs/unit-m-children-field.md` §4/§5.1/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: CHILDREN-HASH-SOURCE — `nodeSource` includes `children` in the fixed field order (after `content`, before `props`), so the SHA-256 hash covers the inline children** | 2026-08-28 | `nodeSource` serializes `children` in the fixed field order (`id, type, content, children, props, ownedNodeIds, createdAt, updatedAt`), so a `children` change → a new hash; a node whose `children` was tampered (changed without a hash update) is QUARANTINED at boot (the hash-verified source discipline). A node WITHOUT `children` hashes identically to a node with `children: undefined` — the ADDITIVE guarantee. | `docs/specs/unit-m-children-field.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: BATCH-ATOMICITY-API — a new `applyBatch(ops)` method on the `RagStore` interface applies a batch of edit operations atomically (a real transaction, not `store.enqueue`)** | 2026-08-28 | The `applyBatch(ops: BatchOp[]): Promise<BatchResult>` method on the `RagStore` interface (`src/main/rag-store.ts`) applies a batch of edit operations ATOMICALLY — all or nothing, with snapshot/restore rollback on failure. The `BatchOp` union is CLOSED at 7 members (the 4 store primitives `putNode`/`removeNode`/`putEdge`/`removeEdge`, applied by Unit N, + the 3 forward-looking rich-text ops `setProps`/`setSubtree`/`setType`, applied by Unit O — a batch containing one is a fail-state in Unit N). A successful batch lands as a SINGLE invertible `batch` journal entry (undo/redo restores the whole batch as a unit) and persists ONCE; a failed batch rolls back the in-memory state to the pre-batch snapshot, does NOT pollute the journal, and does NOT persist. Serialized through the single-writer queue; re-entrant (the `inQueue` pattern, no deadlock). `applyBatch` NEVER throws for a domain failure — it returns the discriminated `BatchResult` (`{ ok: true, results }` / `{ ok: false, error, failedIndex }`). **The RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE must-fix "batch atomicity (a real transaction, not `store.enqueue`)" is now MET (2026-08-28, Unit N).** | `docs/specs/unit-n-batch-atomicity.md` §4/§5.1/§5.2/§5.3/§5.4/§5.5/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: RICH-TEXT-EDIT-OPS — the three rich-text edit ops `setProps`/`setSubtree`/`setType` land on the edit-ops layer, landing the edit-op census 6→9** | 2026-08-28 | `setProps` MERGES props onto a node (only the named keys update; the existing props including the `data-doc-head` marker are preserved — the `setProps` edit op the user chose, Option A); `setSubtree` replaces a node's inline `children` (the Unit M `RagNodeChild[]` field) with a new array (a FULL replace, no merge/append); `setType` changes a node's `type` NEVER delete+create (id/content/children/props/ownedNodeIds preserved; only `type` changes). Each op is a single atomic edit (a single `putNode` write, or a single-op `applyBatch` from Unit N), returns the discriminated `SetPropsResult`/`SetSubtreeResult`/`SetTypeResult`, and NEVER throws for a domain failure. The edit-op census goes from 6 to 9 — the RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE "census 6→9" must-fix is now MET. | `docs/specs/unit-o-edit-ops.md` §4/§5.1/§5.2/§5.3/§5.4/§5.9 |
| **DECIDED: IPC-EDIT-BATCH — the `IPC_EDIT_BATCH` channel is the renderer→main batch channel for the rich-text editing machinery, applied atomically via `applyBatch`** | 2026-08-28 | The `IPC_EDIT_BATCH = 'provident:edit-batch'` channel (a renderer→main `ipcRenderer.invoke` request/reply channel, NOT a fire-and-forget `send`) carries a batch of `BatchOp` values (`EditBatchPayload { ops: BatchOp[] }`) to the RAG store, applied atomically via the `applyBatch` transaction primitive (Unit N) — all or nothing, one `batch` journal entry, one persist. The `handleEditBatch` shared handler (in `src/main/edit-ops.ts`) validates the payload and calls `ragStore.applyBatch`; the `bridge.edit.batch(ops): Promise<BatchResult>` preload method is the renderer-side invocation; the `deriveBatchBroadcast` pure helper derives the `rag-store-changed` payload (`{ kind, nodeIds, edgeIds }`) deterministically from a successful batch. A successful non-empty batch broadcasts `rag-store-changed` EXACTLY ONCE (A4); a failed batch broadcasts 0 times (A2). The channel is MCP/UI-equivalent (§8.2 BINDING) — the same batch reachable via the MCP `edit.batch` tool (forward-looking wiring) and the UI IPC, both routing through the same `applyBatch` primitive. The channel is NOT group-gated (the renderer is a trusted surface) and NOT added to the `RpcMethod` union. | `docs/specs/unit-p-ipc-edit-batch.md` §4/§5.1/§5.2/§5.3/§5.4/§5.5 |

| **DECIDED: NODETEXT-SPACE-JOIN — the retrieval `nodeText(node)` helper returns a node's FULL searchable text as content + every inline child's content, space-joined after dropping empty strings** | 2026-08-28 | The new exported `nodeText(node)` helper in `src/main/retrieval.ts` returns a node's FULL searchable text: the node's `content` + every inline child's `content`, space-joined after dropping empty strings. The three index builders (`createLexicalIndex`/`updateLexicalIndex`/`addToLexicalIndex`) tokenize `nodeText(node)` instead of `node.content`, so the inline `children` text (Unit M) is indexed for retrieval. | `docs/specs/unit-q-retrieval-children-indexing.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: RENDER-DIRECT-CONCAT — the retrieval renderer (`renderNode`/`renderInlineText`) renders content + inline children directly (strong → `**…**`, em → `*…*`, a → `[…](href)`, img → `![alt](src)`)** | 2026-08-28 | The retrieval renderer (`renderNode`/`renderInlineText` in `src/main/retrieval.ts`) renders a node's content + its inline children directly into the searchable text: `strong` → `**…**`, `em` → `*…*`, `a` → `[…](href)`, `img` → `![alt](src)`. Empty-content children are skipped (adversarial F1 — no `****`/`[]()` markers); a non-string `href`/`src` is coerced to string (adversarial F2). | `docs/specs/unit-q-retrieval-children-indexing.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: INLINE-CHILDREN-AUTHORED-ID — the traversal authors each inline child with the id `inline-<ragId>-<index>` (NOT `rag-`-prefixed), distinct from the textarea's `textarea-<ragId>`** | 2026-08-28 | `buildSubtree` (`src/main/traversal.ts`) renders each inline child as a same-type `LegacyNodeData` element (strong/em/a/img) with `content` + merged `props`, authored id `inline-<ragId>-<index>` — NOT `rag-`-prefixed (so `collectSubtreeIds` does NOT treat it as a doc-child subtree root) and distinct from the textarea's `textarea-<ragId>`. Inline children get NO `rag-` id, are NOT in `materialized`, get NO backRefs entry, get NO lineMap range; doc-children ARE separate RAG subtree roots. Ordering: [inline children, textarea overlay, doc-children]. | `docs/specs/unit-r-traversal-inline-children.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: PASTE-SANITIZATION — pasted HTML is sanitized by the pure `sanitizePastedHtml` module into the `RagNodeChild[]` shape, never trusted raw** | 2026-08-28 | A new PURE, node-testable module `src/main/paste-sanitize.ts` exports `sanitizePastedHtml(rawHtml: string): SanitizePasteResult` — a deterministic, TOTAL (never throws for a string input) sanitizer that removes dangerous content and normalizes the surviving content into the `RagNodeChild[]` shape. The discriminated return `{ ok: true; html; content; children } | { ok: false; error }`; the ONLY fail-state is a non-string input. Removes 79 disallowed elements + the `fe*` wildcard + `a`-in-SVG-context; strips `on*`/dangerous-key attributes; validates URLs (http(s), relative, raster-only `data:image/*` for `img`); demotes unsafe/missing-`href` `a` to text, drops unsafe/missing-`src` `img`; folds `span` into the parent's content; hoists nested inline elements to siblings. | `docs/specs/unit-s-paste-sanitization.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: ONE-WAY-SNAPSHOT — the RAG store is a point-in-time copy of the imported markdown corpus; edits go to the RAG store, never back to the source** | 2026-08-28 | The markdown file import (Unit T) makes the RAG store a ONE-WAY SNAPSHOT of the imported corpus (incl. the project's own `docs/*.md` tree). The source markdown files are read-only inputs; the importer performs NO write to them (A4). A subsequent change to a source file does NOT propagate to the RAG store (one-shot, no re-import). This pins the two-source-of-truth externality (amendment 1) as a one-way snapshot — the RAG store is authoritative (RAG-AUTHORITATIVE); the imported markdown is a point-in-time source, not a live feed. | `docs/specs/unit-t-markdown-import.md` §4/§5.4, `docs/specs/markdown-import-review.md` §4 amendment 1 |
| **DECIDED: MARKDOWN-EXPORT-ONLY-CARVE-OUT — initial ingestion is a DISTINCT sanctioned flow that creates RAG nodes/edges from a new corpus, NOT a markdown round-trip edit surface** | 2026-08-28 | Markdown remains EXPORT-ONLY for round-trip edits (MARKDOWN-EXPORT-ONLY). Initial ingestion (Unit T) is a DISTINCT sanctioned flow: it creates RAG nodes/edges from a new corpus and routes through the single-writer store + validation + journal. It does NOT treat the MarkdownAdapter export as an edit surface; the line→node map is a READ/assembly aid, explicitly NOT a write-back path. This is an ADDITIVE refinement of MARKDOWN-EXPORT-ONLY (not a supersession). | `docs/specs/unit-t-markdown-import.md` §4, `docs/specs/markdown-import-review.md` §4 amendment 10 |
| **DECIDED: TABLE-TYPES-ADDITIVE-STORE-FORMAT — `table`/`thead`/`tr`/`td`/`th` are added to `RagNodeType` (18→23) as an additive store-format change** | 2026-08-28 | The markdown file import (Unit T) resolves the table gap IN-SLICE: `table`/`thead`/`tr`/`td`/`th` are added to the `RagNodeType` union (18 → 23 members) and to the store's `RAG_NODE_TYPES` runtime set. The 18 existing members are UNCHANGED; the 5 new members are added. Existing records without these types still load (additive — no migration/re-hash). A GFM pipe table maps to a `table` node; the header row → a `thead` doc-child; each body row → a `tr` doc-child; each cell → a `td`/`th` doc-child. | `docs/specs/unit-t-markdown-import.md` §4/§5.2/§5.8, `docs/specs/markdown-import-review.md` §4 amendment 6 |
| **DECIDED: EDITING-MODE-SETTING — `editingMode: 'textarea' | 'contenteditable'` is an `OperatorSettings` field (default `'contenteditable'`); contenteditable is the DEFAULT editing control; a mode change broadcasts `operator-settings-changed` → the host uses the payload (authoritative) → fresh re-derive** | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-22 at `C9 U-EDIT-1`'s landing by `DECIDED: REPRESENTATION-MODE-SUCCESSOR`** (the `editingMode` field, its `settingsContent` button-toggle and its coerce/label helpers are REMOVED from the tree; the successor names REPRESENTATIONS of one control, not editing controls; a stored legacy `editingMode` is ignored, never trusted — `FS20`) — **SURVIVING CLAUSE (kept verbatim as binding):** the **mode-change broadcast contract** (write → main broadcasts `operator-settings-changed` with the store's result as the authoritative payload → the host uses the payload directly, never a re-fetch → a fresh re-derive, `requestRebuild` → `reDerive`, never `refresh()` over a cached spliced envelope). Original date 2026-08-28 (read `DECIDED: REPRESENTATION-MODE-SUCCESSOR` for the governing rule; this row is retained as provenance) | `editingMode` is an `OperatorSettings` field (default `'contenteditable'`, decision D) + a `settingsContent` button-toggle (text div + button, operator isolated scope, never MCP-visible). Contenteditable is the DEFAULT editing control when `editingMode === 'contenteditable'` (a rich-eligible RAG subtree root splices to `contenteditable: true`; ineligible roots have their textarea removed in contenteditable mode — they render as plain text); textarea is the opt-in legacy control. A mode change writes the operator-settings store → main broadcasts `operator-settings-changed` with the store's result as the authoritative payload → the host uses the PAYLOAD directly (amendment A — no re-fetch, redundant + an async race with the sync `requestRebuild`) → fresh re-derive (`requestRebuild` → `reDerive`, never `refresh()` over the cached spliced envelope). Commit-on-blur, the dirty-edit guard, RAG-authoritative re-traversal, and all-UI-via-provident authoring are RETAINED. **NOTE (commit `1af5000` "Render fix"):** the default was flipped from `'textarea'` to `'contenteditable'`, and in contenteditable mode the textarea is removed for ALL rag roots (not just rich-eligible ones) — ineligible roots render as plain text with no textarea, no `contenteditable` prop, and no `rag-editor-*` handlers. **Control-fallback fix (2026-08-29):** the `settingsContent` button-toggle's `?? 'textarea'` fallback (the `lastOperatorSettings === null` boot-get-failure edge) was changed to `?? 'contenteditable'` so the control never advertises a mode that contradicts the live default; pinned by the extended F1 boot-failure test in `tests/editing-mode-broadcast-host.test.ts`. **Supersedes** FORM-CONTROL-EDITING's "NOT contenteditable (fights graph-is-authoritative; `DomAdapter.text` clobbers a live editor)" clause (line 17) — contenteditable is now the default editing control (the commit-on-blur, dirty-edit guard, and caret/focus-host-state pins remain) — **and** RICH-TEXT-EDITING-GATE's "no global `editingMode` field" clause (line 51) — a global `editingMode` operator setting now exists. **Also supersedes the Unit L `CaretState = { offset; focused }` shape (Unit L §5.4 — a SUPERSEDED decision note, 2026-08-28):** the Unit U4 discriminated `CaretState` (`{kind:'textarea'; offset; focused}` | `{kind:'rich'; ragId; anchor; focus; focused}` with the path-based `RichCaretEdge`) replaces the textarea-only `{ offset; focused }` caret state; the textarea path (`textareaBlur`) now writes `{ kind: 'textarea', offset, focused: dirty }` and the host's re-derive caret-restore loop is kind-GATED (rich caret → contenteditable root, textarea caret → textarea, mismatches DROPPED — amendment 4 / U3 F2 / ADR-8). |  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-editing-mode-toggle-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/decisions.md#EDITING-MODE-SETTING + docs/specs/unit-u1-editing-mode-setting.md … unit-u5-set-rich-text.md) §4-D + §3 amendments 1/2/4; `docs/specs/unit-u1-editing-mode-setting.md`; `docs/specs/unit-u4-contenteditable-editor.md` §1.2 |
| **DECIDED: TEMPLATE-SAVE-BUTTON-REMOVED — the dead Save button is removed from the template-editor pane (M15 refinement)** | 2026-08-28 | The template is AUTO-COMMITTED via the create/delete/reset IPC — there is no separate save IPC and no `editedTemplate` to commit. The template-editor pane's Save button was a dead control (its `template-save` handler was never registered — M15). The Save button is REMOVED from the pane (Unit I): the pane now authors only the zone list + `template-zone-input` + Add-zone + Reset. `template-save` remains NOT registered (the app-graph handler-def census stays 5). This is an ADDITIVE refinement of M15 (the handler was already dropped; now the dead control is gone too). | `docs/specs/unit-i-template.md` §5.4, `docs/specs/unit-k-sidebar-panes-host.md` M15 |
| **DECIDED: SCOPED-LOAD — the document list only finds the document heads; document rendering only walks the graph reachable from the head via the document links (PROPOSAL-GATE, PROCEED-WITH-AMENDMENTS, IMPLEMENTED — all 3 units)** | 2026-08-29 | Fixes the load bug "the app parses the entire graph and times out". The doc-nav derives documents from a lighter `rag-doc-heads` IPC (`{ documents: [{ documentId, title }] }` from the `doc-head` edges + head node content) instead of the full snapshot. `buildTraversal` walks only the reachable subgraph from the head via the document links (`doc-head` → `next-section` → `doc-end` + `doc-child` + `parent-child` for multi-parent) using new `RagStore` adjacency methods (`edgesFrom`/`edgesTo`/`edgesByKind`/`edgesForDocument`/`docHeadForDocument`) + a shared `createSnapshotStore` read-only adapter + a shared `computeDocumentSubgraph` helper (also refactors `rag.get_document`). The scoped walk shrinks `materialized` → `backRefs`/`crosslinks` drop nodes not reachable from the head (an ACCEPTED, regression-tested behavior change). The existing `RagSnapshotPayload` is preserved for `buildTraversal`. **Unit 1 (store adjacency) IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-29)** — the five `RagStore` adjacency methods (`edgesFrom`/`edgesTo`/`edgesByKind`/`edgesForDocument`/`docHeadForDocument`), the lazy O(E) index + invalidation across the 6 mutation paths, the shared PURE adjacency core (`buildAdjacencyIndex` + the 5 query helpers), the quarantine exclusion, and the read-only `createSnapshotStore(nodes, edges)` adapter have landed (the PURE core + `createSnapshotStore` in `src/main/adjacency.ts`, re-exported by `src/main/rag-store.ts`; spec `docs/specs/unit-v1-store-adjacency.md`). **Unit 2 (scoped traversal + MCP refactor) IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-29)** — the scoped `buildTraversal` walk + the shared `computeDocumentSubgraph` helper + the `rag.get_document` refactor + the `rebuildBackRefs`/`buildTraversalEnvelope` adapter replacements have landed. **Unit 3 (doc-heads doc-nav) IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-29)** — the `rag-doc-heads` IPC (`IPC_RAG_DOC_HEADS` + `RagDocHeadsPayload` + the shared `handleRagDocHeadsIpc(store)` handler + the `main.ts` handler + `bridge.rag.docHeads()`) returns `{ documents: [{ documentId, title }] }`; the doc-nav switched to `PaneContext.docHeads` (`deriveDocNavDocuments`/`docNavContent` read `ctx.docHeads`); the host gained the `lastDocHeads` cache + `selectDocument` validates against the doc-heads list (amendment 5); the `RagSnapshotPayload` is preserved for `buildTraversal` (amendment 9). Spec `docs/specs/unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav.md`; greens `docs/specs/unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav-greens.md` 36/36. **LIVE VERIFICATION (2026-08-29):** the boot now renders ONLY the current document (the localeCompare-first doc-head, matching the doc-nav's first entry) instead of the whole corpus — the full-graph render that timed out. `get_rendered_html` dropped from a 60s+ timeout to ~0.15s on the 63-document corpus. (spec `docs/specs/unit-v2-scoped-traversal-mcp.md`; see the SCOPED-WALK / SINGLE-DOCUMENT-SUBGRAPH / MATERIALIZED-SHRINK rows). |  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-load-bug-scoped-traversal-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/decisions.md#SCOPED-LOAD + docs/specs/unit-v1-store-adjacency.md … unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav.md) §5/§6/§7 |
| **DECIDED: ADJACENCY-INDEXED — the `RagStore` exposes the five adjacency methods backed by a lazy O(E) index, invalidated on mutation** | 2026-08-29 | The `RagStore` exposes the five adjacency methods (`edgesFrom`/`edgesTo`/`edgesByKind`/`edgesForDocument`/`docHeadForDocument`) backed by a lazy O(E) index, invalidated on mutation. The index is a pure projection of the store's edges — never a separate source of truth. | `docs/specs/unit-v1-store-adjacency.md` §4 |
| **DECIDED: SHARED-ADJACENCY-CORE — the adjacency semantics live in ONE pure implementation (`buildAdjacencyIndex` + the query helpers)** | 2026-08-29 | The adjacency semantics live in ONE pure implementation (`buildAdjacencyIndex` + the 5 query helpers). The JSON store and `createSnapshotStore` BOTH delegate to it — the renderer's traversal cannot diverge from main's (amendment 3). | `docs/specs/unit-v1-store-adjacency.md` §4 |
| **DECIDED: READ-ONLY-SNAPSHOT-ADAPTER — `createSnapshotStore(nodes, edges)` is a read-only `RagStore` adapter** | 2026-08-29 | `createSnapshotStore(nodes, edges)` is a read-only `RagStore` adapter. Its mutating methods throw (fail-closed — a read-only adapter must not silently accept a write). It is the replacement for the two inline `listNodes`/`listEdges`-only adapters (amendment 4). | `docs/specs/unit-v1-store-adjacency.md` §4 |
| **DECIDED: SCOPED-WALK — `buildTraversal` walks ONLY the reachable subgraph from each document's head via the adjacency methods, never a full-edge scan per document** | 2026-08-29 | `buildTraversal` walks ONLY the reachable subgraph from each document's head via the adjacency methods (`edgesForDocument`/`edgesFrom`/`edgesTo`/`docHeadForDocument`) — never a full-edge scan per document. The walk is O(E) index build (Unit V1) + O(adjacency) lookups. | `docs/specs/unit-v2-scoped-traversal-mcp.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: SINGLE-DOCUMENT-SUBGRAPH — `computeDocumentSubgraph(store, documentId)` is the SINGLE shared derivation of a document's node set, used by BOTH the scoped walk AND the `rag.get_document` MCP tool** | 2026-08-29 | `computeDocumentSubgraph(store, documentId)` is the SINGLE shared derivation of a document's node set + its scoped edges, used by BOTH the scoped `buildTraversal` walk AND the `rag.get_document` MCP tool (amendment 2) — neither re-derives the node set inline, so the traversal and the MCP contract cannot diverge. | `docs/specs/unit-v2-scoped-traversal-mcp.md` §4/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: MATERIALIZED-SHRINK — the scoped walk's `materialized` set is the set of nodes REACHABLE from the head; nodes not reachable are NOT materialized, so `backRefs`/`crosslinks` drop them** | 2026-08-29 | The scoped walk's `materialized` set is the set of nodes REACHABLE from the head (via `doc-head` → `next-section` → `doc-end` + `doc-child` + multi-parent `parent-child`). Nodes not reachable from the head are NOT materialized, so `backRefs`/`crosslinks` drop them. This is a documented, regression-tested behavior change (amendment 1). | `docs/specs/unit-v2-scoped-traversal-mcp.md` §4/§5.4 |
| **DECIDED: DOC-HEADS-IPC — a lighter `rag-doc-heads` IPC returns `{ documents: [{ documentId, title }] }` from the `doc-head` edges + head node content** | 2026-08-29 | A lighter `rag-doc-heads` IPC returns `{ documents: [{ documentId, title }] }` from the `doc-head` edges + head node content. The doc-nav reads this, not the full snapshot. (**entry widened with REQUIRED `path`/`tags` by U-D4, 2026-09-11** — see `docs/specs/unit-ud4-doc-heads-tree.md` §5.1; the IPC constant, payload name, and array shape are unchanged) | `docs/specs/unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: DOC-NAV-DOCHEADS — the doc-nav's `PaneContext` carries `docHeads` (the document list) instead of deriving it from `snapshot`** | 2026-08-29 | The doc-nav's `PaneContext` carries `docHeads` (the document list) instead of deriving it from `snapshot`. `deriveDocNavDocuments`/`docNavContent` read `ctx.docHeads`; `selectDocument` validates against the doc-heads list. | `docs/specs/unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav.md` §4/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: HOST-DOCHEADS-CACHE — the host retains a `lastDocHeads` cache (the doc-heads list) alongside `lastSnapshot`, refreshed on boot/re-derive** | 2026-08-29 | The host retains a `lastDocHeads` cache (the doc-heads list) alongside `lastSnapshot`, refreshed on boot/re-derive. `buildContext()` populates `ctx.docHeads` from it. | `docs/specs/unit-v3-doc-heads-docnav.md` §4/§5.4 |
| **DECIDED: RAG-SNAPSHOT-PRESERVED — the `RagSnapshotPayload` + the `rag-snapshot` IPC are PRESERVED for `buildTraversal` (the rendering half)** | 2026-08-29 | The `RagSnapshotPayload` + the `rag-snapshot` IPC are PRESERVED for `buildTraversal` (the rendering half). | **DECIDED: VECTOR-CACHE-CONTENT-HASH-KEY — the persisted embedding cache: vectors persist in userData keyed by (provider kind, model, dimension, content-hash); embed ONLY on a cache miss; hits adopt with no HTTP call** | 2026-09-05 | The user-approved "vectorize only if the vector is not found" model — the cache-inclusive variant of the vector-boot proposal (the review's parked alternative PROMOTED to IN-SCOPE core contract, superseding the `docs/pending.md` parked row). File `provident-vector-cache.json` in userData (the module-store idiom: atomic temp+rename, one main-process writer). Key = (provider kind, model, dimension, contentHash) where contentHash = lowercase-hex SHA-256 of the exact embedded text (the `nodeSource` SHA-256 discipline). Invalidation = hash mismatch (content changed) OR key mismatch (provider/model/dimension changed) → re-embed. Boot loads the cache (corrupt/unreadable → treated as EMPTY, never crashes boot); the background phase embeds ONLY misses; hits adopt with NO HTTP call; write-through after each successful embed (single-writer queued, atomic temp+rename, not awaited by the embed loop; write failure NON-FATAL); pruning at promotion (drop other-tuple + no-longer-in-store entries). Unit W4 — LANDED 2026-09-05 (spec `docs/specs/unit-f-embeddings.md` §5.13, amended 2026-09-05). **Tests that pin it (ALL LANDED 2026-09-05):** W1 → `tests/vector-boot.test.ts`; W4 → `tests/vector-cache.test.ts` (names SpecWriter-pinned 2026-09-05; W2 → `tests/embeddings-batch.test.ts` —

update the cache"):** the promoted embedder's live embeds route through the

| **DECIDED: MULTI-STORE-REGISTRY — a server-side `provident-rag-stores.json` registry (NOT OperatorSettings) owned by a new pure module `src/main/rag-store-registry.ts`; the implicit `{ name: 'main', default: true }` entry is SYNTHESIZED AT BOOT mapping to the legacy `provident-rag.json` (zero-config byte-equal)** | 2026-09-05 | Home: a NEW server-side file `provident-rag-stores.json` in userData, module-store idiom, sibling of `security-store.ts`/`operator-settings-store.ts` — NOT an `OperatorSettings` field (that store's corrupt→defaults fallback `operator-settings-store.ts:53-63` would silently drop the registry). Shape `{ version: 1, stores: RagStoreConfig[] }`, `RagStoreConfig = { name, default?, persistenceFile?, corpusRoot?, embedder? }`; name charset `/^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/`. Validation (pure, node-testable): unique non-empty names, exactly one `default: true`, no two stores resolving to the same persistence file (derived ∪ explicit), absolute `corpusRoot`/`persistenceFile` when present, `embedder` REJECTED in Phase 1 (fail-loud "per-store embedder config is not supported yet"). Boot split: corrupt/unreadable registry file ⇒ fail-soft (implicit main entry + pinned log); valid-JSON-but-invalid-registry ⇒ fail-LOUD abort before the window. MIGRATION-LEGACY-PATH: the implicit entry is synthesized, never written back; name `main` without `persistenceFile` derives the legacy `provident-rag.json` (not `provident-rag-main.json`) so the first registry write preserves all existing data. Registry mutation is BOOT-TIME-ONLY (D8 — edit the JSON + restart; a mid-run registry change is unobserved until restart, pinned by test). **LANDED (2026-09-05, Unit U-MS2 — spec `docs/specs/unit-ms2-store-wiring.md`, greens 43/0/3, `tests/unit-ms2-store-wiring.test.ts` 62 pass / 1 staged red):** the landed wiring consumes this registry (`main.ts` loads it once at boot; `buildRagStoreDirectory` constructs the N stores + N engines), and validation now ALSO rejects the REGISTRY-FILE self-collision — F14 (F-MS2-1: an entry, derived or explicit, whose persistence path resolves onto the registry file itself fails loud; tests 58–59). **The spec §4 sub-pins recorded WITHIN this row (2026-09-05 doc-review reconciliation — NOT four separate decision rows):** REGISTRY-BOOT-SPLIT (the boot split above), REGISTRY-DERIVED-PATHS (the derived paths + the name-`main` carve-out above), REGISTRY-NO-WRITE (the never-written-back synthesis + boot-time-only mutation + the module's structurally write-free import set), REGISTRY-CWD-TRANSPARENCY (an omitted `corpusRoot` stays OMITTED on the resolved store — the module NEVER fills `process.cwd()` in; the importer's cwd default applies at import time). **LANDED 2026-09-05, Unit U-MS1** — spec `docs/specs/unit-ms1-store-registry.md`, blind greens 35/35 ( `archive/greens/2026-09-21-unit-ms1-store-registry-greens.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor: the owning unit spec + the DONE row carrying the verification; archived basename unit-ms1-store-registry-greens.md), the F-BLIND-MS1-1 BigInt expectation reconciled), `tests/unit-ms1-store-registry.test.ts` 63 tests (63/63 green; the §3a adversarial fix batch = tests 47–63; doc-review pass 2026-09-05). | `docs/specs/multi-document-store-config-review.md` §2 D2/D3/D8 (four-agent gate, user go-ahead 2026-09-05) |
| **DECIDED: SINGLE-WRITER-STORE-PER-STORE — the SINGLE-WRITER-STORE lock-point property is PER-STORE; each `createJsonRagStore` instance keeps its own single-writer queue; no cross-store write transactions** | 2026-08-26 (refined 2026-09-05 for the multi-store slice; marked **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21**) | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21 by `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`** (`docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.1 — the `GN-1` user ruling; **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from the gate record §11.1 + §14.1 and the SUPERSEDED-table enumeration after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]**). The superseded clause is **the per-store queue as the lock point** — **the per-store mapping onto engine wikis is `U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH`'s work** (`docs/specs/unit-authority-switch.md`), which is the P2 prerequisite unit for the cutover. Per-store single-writer is sufficient for the Phase-1 read-only-per-store query surface; NO cross-store write transactions exist (`applyBatch` is per-store — BATCH-ATOMICITY-API), and scratch-store promotion (a cross-store write) is out of scope until an atomicity story exists. **LANDED (2026-09-05, Unit U-MS2 — spec `docs/specs/unit-ms2-store-wiring.md`, greens 43/0/3, `tests/unit-ms2-store-wiring.test.ts` 62 pass / 1 staged red):** the landed directory construction builds the N DISTINCT instances (`buildRagStoreDirectory` rule 1 — each `createJsonRagStore` keeps its own closure queue); greens S08 verified `main.store !== other.store` + cross-store write isolation. | `docs/specs/multi-document-store-config-review.md` §2 D12 (the refinement the feature request itself asked to be recorded) |
