| **DECIDED: TEARDOWN-PRIMITIVES — the U-H5 teardown surface: three `teardown(): Promise<void>` primitives on `RagStore`/`RetrievalEngine`/`VectorBootController` (ASYNC / idempotent NO-OP / no-fail-void, each resolving `undefined` and never throwing/rejecting) + the `RetrievalEngine.inFlight(): number` drain CHECK seam + the OPTIONAL `Embedder.teardown?()` forward hook** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H5** — 22/22 via `tests/unit-h5-teardown.test.ts`; full trio 2687 pass / 41 skip, typecheck + build clean) | Store teardown DRAINS the single-writer queue + revokes MUTATIONS only (reads stay safe; the persistence file is NEVER deleted — D3); the snapshot store's teardown is the reference NO-OP; the engine teardown marks STOPPED + forwards the optional embedder hook (absent on every current embedder = a no-op); the boot-controller teardown GENUINELY CANCELS an in-flight background build (H5-1: an internal abort token + awaiting the REAL build promise so no store/cache/engine mutation outlives teardown) and NEVER deletes/flushes the content-addressed vector-cache file (D3). The torn-down fail-loud set: `rag store: torn down` / `retrieval engine: torn down` / `vector boot: torn down` (+ the unchanged `createSnapshotStore: read-only`). **The seam is wired by U-H4** — the teardown CALL SITES live ONLY in the new `src/main/rag-store-remove.ts` `drainAndReleaseEntry` (the teardown-OWNER module — Q1 option-b); the runtime module `rag-store-runtime.ts` / `main.ts` / `mcp-server.ts` remain UNTOUCHED, so the U-H2/U-H5 N1/A-P2-8 "no `teardown(` call" grep pin STAYS GREEN (no re-pin). U-H6 (hot-rename) / U-H7 (default reassignment) are the remaining consuming callers. | `docs/specs/unit-h5-teardown.md` §4/§5;  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 A-P2-8 / D7 |

| **DECIDED: HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN — the U-H4 drain-then-teardown hot-remove: the runtime controller's ADDITIVE async `hotRemove(name): Promise<HotRemoveResult>` in `rag-store-runtime.ts` + the NEW node-testable teardown-OWNER module `src/main/rag-store-remove.ts` (`drainAndReleaseEntry`)** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H4** — 20/20 via `tests/unit-h4-hot-remove.test.ts`; co-existing `tests/unit-h2-runtime-controller.test.ts` 56/56 + `tests/unit-h5-teardown.test.ts` 22/22; full trio **2707 pass / 41 skip**, typecheck + build clean — the Implementer's run) | `hotRemove` REUSES the LANDED synchronous `hotApply({kind:'remove'})` for the write + default-stability + live-map swap (the orphan `RagStoreEntry` is captured BEFORE the swap), then AWAITS `drainAndReleaseEntry(orphan)`. The runtime module KEEPS its N1/A-P2-8 no-lowercase-`teardown(` grep pin GREEN (Q1 option-b — the teardown CALL SITES live ONLY in `rag-store-remove.ts`, which is the teardown-OWNER module; `rag-store-remove.ts` imports NO `unlink`/`rm`/`rmdir` — D3). The drain is UNBOUNDED (`while (engine.inFlight() !== 0) await sleep(1)`, re-entered immediately before `engine.teardown()` per **F-H4-1**; a genuinely never-settling query/queue hangs `hotRemove` — F-H5-3 awareness, not a bounded fail-state). D3 strand — the persistence file + journal are NEVER deleted/touched, byte-identical after every path. Default-stable (the default store object identity carries across); default removal is loader-**F12**-rejected; an unknown/already-removed name fails loud with **W-remove-unknown**; a bad name (`null`/`5`/`''`) hits the locally-thrown byte-equal **W-remove-arg** guard. Adversarial **F-H4-1** (drain-gate TOCTOU) / **F-H4-2** (default-drift throw-path orphan leak — a removed-but-drifted store is still drained before the error rethrows) FIXED + regression-tested; F-H4-3/4/5 INFO recorded. New byte-pinned message templates: **0**. The operator-facing trigger + confirmation dialog is **U-H8**'s — the mechanism lands here, node-testable (D6/A-P2-7). | `docs/specs/unit-h4-hot-remove.md` §4/§5 + the review  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 D3/D7/A-P2-2/A-P2-8 + the doc-review `archive/reviews/2026-09-08-unit-h4-doc-review.md` |

| **DECIDED: HOT-RENAME-DRAIN-TEARDOWN — the U-H6 drain-then-teardown hot-rename: the runtime controller's ADDITIVE async `hotRename(from, to): Promise<HotRenameResult>` in `rag-store-runtime.ts`, MIRRORING U-H4's `hotRemove` by REUSING the LANDED `src/main/rag-store-remove.ts` `drainAndReleaseEntry` (NO new module, NO new teardown call site)** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H6** — 20/20 via `tests/unit-h6-hot-rename.test.ts` (14 new + 6 pins); the co-existing U-H4/U-H2/U-H5 suites stay green 20/56/22; full trio **2727 pass / 41 skip**, typecheck + build clean) | `hotRename(from, to)` REUSES the LANDED synchronous `hotApply({kind:'rename'})` for the write + default-stability + live-map swap (the old-`from` orphan `RagStoreEntry` is CAPTURED BEFORE the swap), then AWAITS `drainAndReleaseEntry({ store: orphan.store, engine: orphan.engine })`. The runtime KEEPS its N1/A-P2-8 no-lowercase-`teardown(` grep pin GREEN (no literal `teardown(` — `hotRename` references only the identifier `drainAndReleaseEntry(`; the teardown CALL SITES stay the LANDED U-H4 4, all in `rag-store-remove.ts`; **U-H6 adds ZERO new call sites in the runtime**). The **D4 no-ids precondition is INHERITED** from `hotApply` (the `R-rename-ids-present` inspect runs first inside `hotApply`, skipped for a default rename via HOST-2; `hotRename` does NOT re-inspect). The drain is **UNBOUNDED** (`inFlight()===0`, re-entered immediately before `engine.teardown()` per the inherited **F-H4-1**; a never-settling query/queue hangs `hotRename` — F-H5-3 awareness). **D3 strand** — the old `from` persistence file + journal are NEVER deleted/touched, byte-identical after every path; the new `to` entry is rebuilt on its NEW derived file. **NON-DEFAULT-only** — a default rename propagates the write module's **W-rename-default** and FOLDS INTO **U-H7** (default reassignment; the D4 inspect is skipped for the default rename so W-rename-default fires first); unknown/renamed-away `from` → **W-rename-unknown-from**; present `to` → **W-rename-target-exists**; bad `from`/`to` (`null`/`5`/`''`) → the locally-thrown byte-equal **W-rename-arg** `from required`/`to required` guard (0 new templates). The **F-H4-2** default-drift orphan-leak drain (a renamed-but-drifted old store is still drained before the byte-pinned rethrow) is inherited + regression-tested. Adversarial (RCA-3): **NO host defects**; INFO-H6-1 (`from===to` → W-rename-target-exists, untested edge, consistent), INFO-H6-2 (whitespace names are spec-conformant module-wide, do NOT change), INFO-H6-3 (survivor-rebuild-orphan churn, pre-existing U-H2 behavior out of scope). New byte-pinned message templates: **0**. The operator-facing trigger + confirmation dialog is **U-H8**'s (D6). | `docs/specs/unit-h6-hot-rename.md` §4/§5;  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 **D4**/**D5**/**A-P2-5**/**A-P2-8**; the review D4/D5/A-P2-5/A-P2-8 |

| **DECIDED: HOT-SET-DEFAULT — the U-H7 runtime default reassignment: the controller's ADDITIVE async `hotSetDefault(name): Promise<HotSetDefaultResult>` in `rag-store-runtime.ts` (the default becomes RE-BINDABLE — `defaultEntry`/`vectorBoot` const→let, re-pointed on reassignment) + the NEW default-change orchestration home `src/main/rag-store-default.ts` (`createDefaultVectorBoot`/`releaseDefaultVectorBoot`)** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H7** — 29/29 via `tests/unit-h7-default-reassign.test.ts`; blind-greens 18 PASS / 0 FAIL / 4 DEFERRED; full trio **2761 pass / 41 skip**, typecheck + build clean) | `hotSetDefault` flips `default:true` onto an existing non-default store via the write module's additive `setDefault` kind (D2 — `writeRegistryMutation`), RE-BINDS `getDefaultName`/`getDefaultEntry`/`getDefaultStore`/`getDefaultEngine`/`getVectorBoot`/`getDirectory().defaultName` (A-P2-1 — every already-rewired closure reflects the new default per call; closes U-H2 **HOST-LOW-1** on this path, §7 Q11), and in vector mode re-warms the NEW default via **construct-first** then releases the OLD default's boot LAST (`releaseDefaultVectorBoot` — the UNBOUNDED `inFlight()===0` drain + F-H4-1 no-await re-entry + `boot.teardown()`) with a **RETAIN-old-default** policy (the OLD default's STORE + persistence file + journal strand byte-identical, D3; its vector engine is replaced in place by a fresh lexical engine — Q4). The `setDefault`/`renameDefault` deltas carry the OPTIONAL **`RegistryDelta.defaultChanged?`** member (`[name]` / `[to]`; ABSENT on add/remove/rename so the U-H2/U-H4/U-H6 3-key exact-delta assertions stay green). The runtime module KEEPS its **N1/A-P2-8 `no teardown(` grep pin** (ZERO call sites — it references only `createDefaultVectorBoot(`/`releaseDefaultVectorBoot(`; the sole genuine `boot.teardown()` call site lives in `rag-store-default.ts`, +1 total). A same-default call is a NO-OP (`noop:true`, no write/teardown); an unknown target fails loud via **W-set-default-unknown**; a native fs failure propagates with live+disk untouched. The operator-facing trigger + confirmation + accessor-backed IPC are **U-H8**'s (D6 — mechanism-only). | `docs/specs/unit-h7-default-reassign.md` §4 (DEFAULT-REASSIGNMENT-METHOD, DEFAULT-REBIND, SET-DEFAULT-WRITE-KIND, DEFAULT-CHANGE-KEEPS-STORE, VECTOR-REWARM-CONSTRUCT-FIRST, ATOMIC-APPLY-DEFAULT) + §5.1/§5.3/§5.4; the review  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 D1/D5/A-P2-1/A-P2-2/A-P2-4/A-P2-8 |

| **DECIDED: DEFAULT-RENAME-PRESERVES-DEFAULT — the U-H7 SANCTIONED default rename (D4 fold, §7 Q3 **R1**): a SEPARATE write-module kind + a `hotRenameDefault(to)` controller seam that renames the CURRENT default while it STAYS the default; the LEGACY `W-rename-default` guard is NOT relaxed** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H7**) | The default's rename folds into U-H7 as a NEW, first-class sanctioned op — `{ kind: 'renameDefault'; to }` + the `renameDefaultRegistryStore` convenience (renames the current default preserving `default:true`) + **`hotRenameDefault(to): Promise<HotRenameDefaultResult>`** (the controller's 14th member; `delta.renamed === [{from,to}]` AND `delta.defaultChanged === [to]`). The D4 **persisted-`<from>:`-id decline** (R-rename-ids-present, RCA-3 HOST-2) runs BEFORE the write (disk+live untouched, no drain). The legacy `hotApply({kind:'rename'})`/`hotRename` DEFAULT paths KEEP propagating **W-rename-default** (U-H2 F9/HOST-2 + U-H6 F4 stay green — R2 fold-in / R3 guard-relax REJECTED). The default's config keeps its OTHER pinned fields verbatim (`persistenceFile` RE-DERIVED under the new name if omitted; corpusRoot preserved); the OLD default's derived file + journal strand byte-identical (D3, never migrated/deleted). RCA-3 host fixes recorded: HOST-3 (the F7-consistent defensive drift guard, sync-to-loaded before R-default-changed), HOST-4 (the whitespace-`to` guard → `to required`), HOST-5 (the `reassignInFlight` concurrency serialization → `rag-store-runtime: default reassignment already in progress`), HOST-1 (re-point `defaultPersistenceFile` after the rename). | `docs/specs/unit-h7-default-reassign.md` §4 (DEFAULT-RENAME-FOLD) + §5.1/§5.4/H8/F6/F8; the review  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 D4 / A-P2-5 |

| **DECIDED: OPERATOR-UI-REGISTRY-MANAGE — the ONE `IPC_RAG_STORE_MANAGE` operator channel (the review §2 D6's sole exemption) + the shared `handleRagStoreManageIpc` two-phase handler wrapping the LANDED hot-* seams + the provident-authored operator-scope manage pane (INLINE-only bodies — NEVER globally handler-registered, so an agent CANNOT drive it)** | 2026-09-08 (**LANDED, Unit U-H8** — 64/64 via `tests/unit-h8-operator-editor.test.ts`; blind-greens 23 PASS / 0 FAIL / 6 DEFERRED / 6 not-live-runnable; full trio **2825 pass / 41 skip**, typecheck + build clean) | The D6 exemption = EXACTLY ONE `IPC_RAG_STORE_MANAGE = 'provident:rag-store-manage'` mutating channel (op-discriminated `RagStoreManageOp`/`RagStoreManageRequest`/`RagStoreManageResult`; one channel const + three shared types). The shared, node-testable `handleRagStoreManageIpc(runtime, request)` reads the live projection per call (`getDefaultName()`/`currentStores()`/`statusOf` — A-P2-1) and runs the two-phase confirmation: the DESTRUCTIVE ops (`remove`/`rename`/`setDefault`/`renameDefault`) return `{ confirmationRequired: true, summary }` WITHOUT `confirmed:true` (NO seam), and the same op with `confirmed:true` invokes the LANDED `hotApply add`/`hotRemove`/`hotRename`/`hotSetDefault`/`hotRenameDefault`, whose own rejection PROPAGATES fail-closed; `add` is immediate (non-destructive). Operator-only, NOT group-gated (IPC-SURFACE-NOT-GROUP-GATED), NOT MCP-visible (D6/A-P2-6/A-P2-7). **HOST-H8-1 (HIGH — the security vector, CLOSED):** the operator-scope manage bodies are carried INLINE (full-expression bodies, both `name` + `body`) and NEVER registered in the global app-graph `handlerDef` table — the pre-fix `registerRagManageHandlerDefs` (`pane-graph.ts` → `registerHandlerDef`) let a `code.set`-crafted template name-resolve the destructive `operator-rag-manage-confirm` body in the app Runtime (`resolveNameReferencedHandlerBodies`) + drive `provident.dispatch` without operator confirmation (falsifying A-P2-6/A-P2-7); removing the global registration leaves the operator-UI IPC surface truly operator-only + agent-un-driveable. HOST-H8-2/3/4/5 (confirm-step default/target re-guard, request-step fail-safe umbrella, in-flight `regMgmtConfirmingOp` guard, null/undefined throw-stringify guard) all fixed + regression-tested. NO new MCP tool / `RpcMethod`/`TOOL_GROUPS`/`ALL_TOOLS`/`MUTATING_METHODS` member and NO `security.ts` group-gate row — the MCP tool census stays **41**; the operator pane is operator-only + agent-un-driveable; the operator trigger + confirmation are now WIRED. | `docs/specs/unit-h8-operator-editor.md` §4/§5 (OPERATOR-MANAGE-IPC / CONFIRMATION-CLASS / TWO-PHASE-CONFIRM / OPERATOR-MANAGE-PANE / the §7 Q1–Q7 ruling) + the review  `archive/gate-reviews/2026-09-21-registry-hot-apply-review.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor docs/specs/unit-h1-registry-write.md … unit-h8-operator-editor.md (the landed U-H1..U-H8 units) + docs/decisions.md#HOT-REMOVE-DRAIN-TEARDOWN) §2 D6/D3/D7/D8/A-P2-6/A-P2-7 |

| **DECIDED: ENGINE-WIRE-CLIENT — the shell's Gnosis-engine access is a NEW module `src/main/engine-rag-store.ts` exporting the factory `createEngineRagStore(opts)` returning the retrieval-trio + health proxy surface** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN** — 70/70 unit, 63/63 blind-greens, trio 2958 pass / 41 skip) | The proxy generalizes Incanter's `createRemoteRagStore` (the pending `docs/pending.md` "remote RAG store" row) but is a DISTINCT surface from the Astrographer `RagStore` interface (Unit A §5.4 — a node/edge CRUD store). The proxy is the retrieval trio + health ONLY (`ragQuery`/`ragStream`/`getEngineStatus` + `health`/`waitForReady`); it does NOT implement the `RagStore` CRUD interface. Full `RagStore` CRUD routing, the server-host binary crate, and CRUD wire shapes are DEFERRED (Architecture A1). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: TRANSPORT-HTTP-SSE-LOOPBACK — the transport is HTTP/REST + SSE over loopback (`127.0.0.1`); the endpoint paths are pinned as constants** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | The proxy issues REST calls for `ragQuery`/`getEngineStatus`/`health` and subscribes to SSE for `ragStream`. The endpoint paths are pinned as `ENGINE_ENDPOINTS` (`/rag/query` POST, `/rag/stream` GET-SSE, `/engine/status` GET). The server host is a separate thin binary crate in the Gnosis workspace (deferred — out of scope). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §4/§5.1 |
| **DECIDED: READY-OBSERVATION — the shell OBSERVES the engine boot→READY lifecycle via `getEngineStatus` (the `state` + `subsystems` flags) and gates RAG calls on `state == Ready`; the shell does NOT drive engine-internal boot** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | Each RAG call (`ragQuery`/`ragStream`) issues a FRESH `getEngineStatus` immediately before proceeding (a deterministic function of the observed state — P-SM-2). A non-`Ready` state → `EngineUnavailable` (`cause:'not-ready'` for `Starting`/`Degraded`, `cause:'unavailable-state'` for `Unavailable`). `Degraded` is a gate, not an error to observe (a faithful projection). The proxy exposes `getEngineStatus`/`health` + a `waitForReady()` helper. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §4/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-RAG-RESULT-TRACE — the proxy returns the proxy-specific `EngineRagResult`/`EngineRagTrace` types (the wire body mapped to a shell-consumable shape), NOT the Astrographer `RagResult`/`RagTrace`** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | The wire body carries none of the local fan-out's `ranked`/`context`/`markdown`/`lineMap`/`k` fields, and the wire `trace` covers all four engine modes (flat/vector/graph/hybrid) whereas the Astrographer `RagTrace` is flat/graph ONLY. The proxy therefore returns the proxy-specific `EngineRagResult` (with the `mode`-discriminated `EngineRagTrace` union), preserving the full wire trace. `RagQueryFilters`/`BlockedByEntry` are IMPORTED from `src/main/retrieval.ts` (Unit X §5.2) — NOT new types of this unit. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §4/§5.1/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: WAIT-FOR-READY-SHELL-CONVENIENCE — `waitForReady` is a shell-side convenience (it composes `getEngineStatus` polling), NOT a wire method** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | The proxy surface method count is 5 (`ragQuery`/`ragStream`/`getEngineStatus`/`health`/`waitForReady`); the retrieval trio + health = 4, and `waitForReady` is the READY-observation helper — a shell-side convenience that polls `getEngineStatus` every `pollIntervalMs` (default 500) up to `maxAttempts` (default 60), resolving to the `HealthReport` whose `state == 'Ready'`. It keeps polling through `Starting` AND `Degraded` (both non-terminal); a connection-refused during polling propagates immediately (no retry). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §5.1/§5.6/§5.10 |
| **DECIDED: ASSERT-LOOPBACK-INTERNAL — the loopback check helper `assertLoopback` is INTERNAL (NOT exported); loopback enforcement is tested through the factory throw pattern** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | `assertLoopback` is a private helper in `src/main/engine-rag-store.ts`; loopback enforcement is tested through the factory throw pattern (§5.9 fail-states 1–3: `opts required` / `baseUrl required` / `baseUrl must be loopback`). The factory is synchronous (no network I/O at construction), so DNS resolution is not performed — `localhost` is accepted as the loopback alias per §5.8-14 (H11 documented limitation). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §5.1/§5.10 |
| **DECIDED: TRANSPORT-CAUSE-MAPPING — the transport-path `cause` mapping: connection-refused → `EngineUnavailable` `cause:'connection-refused'`; engine-not-spawned → `cause:'engine-not-spawned'`; the wire-code path assigns `cause:'unavailable-state'` (shared with the gate path)** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN**) | The D2 engine-absent split is deterministic: a fetch/SSE connection-refused (`ECONNREFUSED`) → `EngineUnavailable` (503, `cause:'connection-refused'`); engine-not-spawned (`ENOTFOUND`) → `EngineUnavailable` (503, `cause:'engine-not-spawned'`); any other transport failure → the typed `EngineWireError` (H6). `wireCodeToError('engine_unavailable', msg)` returns the `EngineUnavailable` subclass with `cause:'unavailable-state'` — the ONE cause the wire-code path assigns, SHARED with the §5.6 gate path (the gate ALSO assigns `cause:'unavailable-state'` when it observes the engine's `'Unavailable'` state). The `'connection-refused'`/`'engine-not-spawned'`/`'not-ready'` causes are assigned ONLY by the shell-side transport/gate paths. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-engine-integration.md` §4/§5.4/§5.6/§5.9 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-CRUD-SURFACE-CONFIRMED — the app's document/graph CRUD MCP/UI actions ARE the intended future surface for the Gnosis `RagStore` CRUD routing (D4 parity at the shell, via the proxy seam)** | 2026-09-10 (**ACTIVE** — pins current intent) | **Gnosis is the external graph-RAG / document-store engine.** The Astrographer app's MCP/UI actions that perform CRUD on documents and/or graph operations are therefore the **intended future surface for the Gnosis `RagStore` CRUD routing** — that surface should expose the Gnosis document + graph CRUD (the deferred full `RagStore` CRUD routing) on BOTH the GUI and the MCP surface (D4 parity), consistent with the proxy seam (`createEngineRagStore`). **Currently DEFERRED/blocked** because (1) the Gnosis F2 wire contract froze ONLY the retrieval trio + health — there are NO frozen CRUD wire shapes (the shell must NOT invent them unilaterally), and (2) the `gnosis-server` binary crate is deferred (no live engine endpoint). The current retrieval-trio wiring unit `unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring` (`docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md`) is the correct FIRST wiring increment; the CRUD surface is a SEPARATE, later unit gated on the Gnosis crate freezing the 45-method `RagStore` CRUD wire shapes + the `gnosis-server` binary crate. | user confirmation 2026-09-10 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-TOOL-NAMING-GROUP (A1) — the three wiring tools are `gnosis.query`/`gnosis.stream`/`gnosis.status` (`gnosis.*`, NOT `rag.*`/`engine.*`), in a NEW read-only default-off `gnosis` `ToolGroup` union member** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.2) | A NEW `ToolGroup` union member `'gnosis'` (read-only, default-off) is added to `security.ts` — the union (line 3) becomes `'read'|'dispatch'|'graph'|'code'|'module'|'rag'|'edit'|'gnosis'`; three `TOOL_GROUPS` rows (`'gnosis.query'`/`'gnosis.stream'`/`'gnosis.status'` → `'gnosis'`); `'gnosis'` added to `VALID_GROUPS`. `defaultSecurityConfig()` is UNCHANGED (the default enabled set stays `['read','dispatch']` — `gnosis` is OFF by default, a human enables it via the manual-UI settings pane, like `rag`/`edit`/`code`/`module`). The three names are added to `ALL_TOOLS` (`mcp-server.ts:1521-1528`). A `gnosis.*` name NOT in `TOOL_GROUPS` → `null` (fail-closed). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.2 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-MAIN-ROUTING-AUDIT (A2) — the `gnosis.*` tools route to a MAIN-process handler (`handleGnosisTool`); `gnosis.query`/`gnosis.stream` record to the shared audit log (like `rag.query`); `gnosis.status` is read-only and does NOT record; `McpServerOptions.engineRagStore?` is injected like `retrievalEngine`** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.3) | The `mcp-server.ts` main-handled branch (`~:1989`, `name.startsWith('gnosis.')`) routes to the exported `handleGnosisTool(engine, name, args, auditLog?)`. `gnosis.query`/`gnosis.stream` record the SAME `QueryAuditEntry` shape as `rag.query` (`query`/`filters`/`mode`/`resultCount`/`timestamp`/`requester:'mcp'`); `gnosis.status` records NONE. `auditLog` null/absent → skip (no throw). Unknown `gnosis.*` name → `Error('unknown gnosis tool: <name>')`. **`QueryAuditEntry.mode` widening (§5.3, the ONE change to `src/main/query-audit.ts`):** `'flat'|'graph'` → `'flat'|'graph'|'vector'|'hybrid'` (the gnosis tools pass the proxy's full four-member mode set; existing `rag.query`/`rag-stream` entries stay byte-equal). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.3 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-STREAM-OVER-MCP (A3) — `gnosis.stream` fully consumes the proxy's single-shot `AsyncIterable<RagChunk>` within the tool call and returns `{ chunks: RagChunk[] }`; the READY gate fires INSIDE the call (a non-`Ready` state surfaces as the typed `EngineUnavailable`, a tool-level throw — NOT an error chunk)** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.3) | `gnosis.stream` collects all chunks into an array and returns `{ chunks }` (`[{type:'result',result}]` or `[{type:'error',code,message}]` then `[{type:'done'}]`, in order) — a FULL consumption (SSE teardown + premature-close `EngineUnavailable` are handled by the proxy's own consumption; the tool leaves NO half-open stream). Single-shot, bounded by the proxy's `requestTimeoutMs` (10s default). The audit entry's `resultCount` = the single `result` chunk's `result.results.length` (0 for an `error` chunk). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.3 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-ENGINE-LIFECYCLE (A4) — the proxy is constructed at boot UNCONDITIONALLY (no I/O at construction) and injected into `McpServerOptions.engineRagStore`; the `baseUrl` comes from a config seam → env → default; `waitForReady` is NOT awaited at boot and NOT exposed as a tool** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.4) | `main.ts` constructs `createEngineRagStore({ baseUrl: resolveEngineBaseUrl() })` unconditionally at boot (construction does no I/O) and injects it into `ProvidentMcpServer` as `engineRagStore`. `resolveEngineBaseUrl()` is the pinned 3-step priority: (1) the config seam — the NEW `src/main/engine-config.ts` (a persisted, non-credential engine `baseUrl` store; the in-memory registry `setEngineConfigBaseUrl`/`getEngineConfigBaseUrl`; config value wins), (2) the env var `PROVIDENT_ENGINE_BASE_URL`, (3) the default `http://127.0.0.1:8080`. The resolved `baseUrl` MUST be loopback (the proxy throws at construction on a non-loopback address). `waitForReady` is a shell-side convenience, NOT awaited at boot and NOT a D4-parity tool. `baseUrl` is env/CLI config — NOT a credential. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.4 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-D4-PARITY-SCOPE (A5) — the first wiring unit is scoped to the three MCP tools + a status OPERATOR pane + a query APP-GRAPH pane, both provident-authored, with a NEW `EngineRagResult` render path** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.5) | The status screen is the `gnosis-status` OPERATOR pane (isolated `createIsolatedScope()` GraphScope → MCP-INVISIBLE; keeps engine state out of `get_rendered_html`); the query screen is the `gnosis-query` APP-GRAPH pane (MCP-visible like the `search` pane), gated FAIL-CLOSED on the `gnosis` group. Both authored as provident data (envelope nodes + function-string handler bodies), never hand-written DOM. NEW pure render helpers `gnosisQueryContent(ctx, result)` (renders the proxy-specific `EngineRagResult`, DISTINCT from the local `RagResult`'s `ranked`/`context`/`markdown`/`lineMap`/`k`) and `gnosisStatusContent(ctx, report)`. Host `GnosisPanes` (`src/renderer/gnosis-panes.ts`) registers/enables both panes + the handler defs; the two panes are registered by `GnosisPanes`, NOT by `SidebarPanes.registerPanes()`, so the Unit H census of exactly 5 panes stays intact. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.5 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-D2-ENGINE-ABSENT (A6) — `EngineUnavailable` is surfaced consistently: the MCP tools throw the typed `EngineWireError` (code + httpStatus 503); the GUI status pane shows the unavailable state; the happy path uses the proxy's injectable `fetch`/`sse`** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.6) | A connection-refused engine → `EngineUnavailable` (503, `cause:'connection-refused'`); a non-`Ready` observed state → `EngineUnavailable` (503, `cause:'not-ready'` for `Starting`/`Degraded`, `cause:'unavailable-state'` for `Unavailable`); a malformed wire body → `EngineError` (502) / `TraceUnavailable` (502). The GUI status pane renders the unavailable state on a null report or a rejected bridge call (never a crash). The happy path is tested through the real proxy with an injected mock `fetch`/`sse`; the engine-absent path via connection-refused → `EngineUnavailable`. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.6 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-SECURITY-CARVE-OUT (A7) — the GUI-only boundary is enumerated precisely; `baseUrl` is NOT a credential; NO `gnosis.*` tool `inputSchema` accepts a credential arg (no `token`/`tls`/`ca`/`cert`/`key`/`apiKey`)** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.2/§5.7) | GUI-only at the shell (never MCP-tool args, never MCP-visible): the engine credentials (the proxy's `auth.token` bearer + the TLS `ca`/`cert`/`key`), Astral push creds, and Firmament bridge auth. `baseUrl` is env/CLI config (A4), NOT a credential and NOT an MCP tool arg. A test asserts NONE of the three `gnosis.*` tools' `inputSchema` accepts a credential field (P-IM-1). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.2/§5.7 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-HEALTH-AS-TOOL (A8) — `gnosis.status` is a TOOL in the `gnosis` group (default-off), NOT an `mcp://` resource (a resource would be gated on the default-ON `read` group, leaking engine status)** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.1/§5.2) | `gnosis.status` is registered as an MCP TOOL in the `gnosis` group (default-off), NOT as an `mcp://` resource. The `gnosis` group is the ONLY gate on engine status (reading the `HealthReport`); a `read`-group agent cannot reach it. Read-only; no audit entry. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.1/§5.2 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-CONTRACT-SPEC-UPDATE (A9) — the three `gnosis.*` tools + the `gnosis` group (default-off) are recorded in the endpoint spec; the `gnosis` group + the GNOSIS decision rows are recorded in `docs/decisions.md`** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.11) | A9 is satisfied by (a) the GNOSIS decision rows recorded here, (b) the `gnosis` group row added to `mcp-endpoint.md` §6.2 (default OFF), and (c) the exhaustive contract pinned in `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` itself (§5.1–§5.11). **NOTE (this doc review):** `mcp-endpoint.md` §3 is the base `provident.*` tool table (it does not host the Astrographer `rag.*`/`edit.*`/`module.*` tools either — those live in their unit specs + `docs/decisions.md`), so the three `gnosis.*` tools are NOT appended to the §3 `provident.*` table (doing so would be structurally inconsistent); the app-specific tool catalog's authoritative home is the unit spec + this decisions.md record. `D4-MCP-GUI-PARITY` is a guide §9 concept, NOT a decisions.md row (verified: no such row exists). | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.11 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-GUI-BRIDGE-IPC — the GUI's two panes reach main via the `gnosisStatus`/`gnosisQuery` bridge methods + the `IPC_GNOSIS_STATUS`/`IPC_GNOSIS_QUERY` IPC channels, routed to the SAME `handleGnosisTool` handler as the `gnosis.*` MCP tools (MCP/UI equivalence)** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit GN-MCP-UI** — spec §5.5, HOST-MAJOR-2) | The bridge surface is `window.provident.sidebar.gnosisStatus`/`gnosisQuery` → `bridge.gnosis.status/query` (preload) → the `IPC_GNOSIS_STATUS`/`IPC_GNOSIS_QUERY` IPC channels (`shared/types.ts:680-681`) → MAIN → the SAME `handleGnosisTool`. The GUI handler bodies reach the bridge via `window.provident.sidebar` (the M2 pattern) — NEVER an MCP tool. `gnosis.status`/`gnosis.query` IPC paths are NOT group-gated (the renderer is a trusted surface — IPC-SURFACE-NOT-GROUP-GATED), but the query pane's handler body itself gates fail-closed on the `gnosis` group. | `docs/specs/unit-gn-mcp-ui-wiring.md` §5.5/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-SUPPLANTS-DOCUMENT-STORE — Gnosis supplants the existing document store of Astrographer; the A2 document-CRUD screens ARE the document surface over the engine (no second editor, no sync); the local `createJsonRagStore` becomes the D2 fallback when the engine is absent** | 2026-09-10 (**ACTIVE** — pins current intent) | The intended architecture is that the RAG data container is the Provident graph, and Gnosis IS the Provident graph engine. Pointing Astrographer at Gnosis is the correct architecture, not a divergence (verified: the local store is already graph-based — `RagNode` is "one knowledge-graph object. OWNS a subtree of provident nodes", `rag-store.ts:98` — so there is NO drift/defect to record). The A2 document-CRUD screens are the document surface over the engine; the local `createJsonRagStore` is the D2 fallback when the engine is absent (gnosis.md §4.6.2 "the shell's document store and cross-link features work fully" without the engine). Pointing at Gnosis is NOT a local-first violation because Gnosis and Astrographer ship together and run on the same machine (a co-shipped local process, not a remote dependency). | user decision 2026-09-10 (proposal gate, H1) |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-CRUD-MVP-SCOPE — the Astrographer MVP needs only document CRUD (not community/fact access); the CRUD roadmap's MVP is document-CRUD only (P1a + P2 + A1 + A2), with graph-ops/fact/consistency wiring (A3/A4/A5) and the graph/fact/consistency/RAG-companion wire units (P1b–P1e) deferred as follow-ons, NOT gates** | 2026-09-10 (**ACTIVE** — pins current intent) | The MVP surface is the 11 §4.1 document-CRUD methods over the frozen document-CRUD wire shapes (P1a) + the `gnosis-server` binary crate (P2) + the document-CRUD routing proxy client (A1) + the document CRUD D4 wiring (A2). A3 (graph-ops /18), A4 (fact/citation /6), A5 (consistency /4) and P1b–P1e are deferred follow-ons. | user decision 2026-09-10 (proposal gate, MVP scope) |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-RBAC-EDIT-ENFORCEMENT — Gnosis accepts a credential on the mutating CRUD calls to confirm whether the caller has edit access to make the given change (the engine is the RBAC enforcer, enforcing the intended RBAC locking); Astrographer stores the authority of its human/agent users and presents the caller's authority credential on each mutating call** | 2026-09-10 (**ACTIVE** — pins current intent) | The mutating `gnosis.document.*` tools sit in a separate mutating default-off group (e.g. `gnosis-edit`, per RAG-EDIT-MCP-GROUPS), distinct from the read-only `gnosis` group. Gnosis accepts a **credential** on the mutating CRUD calls to confirm whether the caller has edit access to make the given change — the engine is the RBAC **enforcer** (the intended RBAC locking). Astrographer **stores the authority of its human/agent users** (the RBAC mapping of who may edit) and presents the caller's authority credential on each mutating call. This is distinct from the transport auth (token/TLS, GUI-only per the A7 carve-out): the RBAC credential is the caller's edit-authority, checked by the engine. The P1a wire contract must pin the RBAC credential shape; the A2 unit must wire the shell's authority store to the engine's RBAC check. | user decision 2026-09-10 (proposal gate, H3) |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-CRUD-WIRE-CLIENT — the shell's Gnosis document-CRUD access is a NEW module `src/main/engine-crud-rag-store.ts` exporting the factory `createEngineCrudRagStore(opts)` returning the 11-method document-CRUD proxy surface** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A1** — spec §4/§5.1) | The 11-method document-CRUD proxy surface (`createDocument`/`getDocument`/`updateDocument`/`deleteDocument`/`publishDocument`/`unpublishDocument`/`archiveDocument`/`listDocuments`/`createWiki`/`getWiki`/`listWikis`) over the frozen P1a wire. It is a **sibling** to `engine-rag-store.ts` (Unit GN) and **imports** the shared error model, status map, and decode helpers from it (do not redefine). It is a **distinct surface** from the Astrographer `RagStore` interface (Unit A §5.4) and from the retrieval-trio proxy (Unit GN). 7 mutating methods require `caller`; 4 read-only methods carry no `caller` (RBAC-CALLER-THREADING). 11 endpoints (`ENGINE_CRUD_ENDPOINTS`), pairwise distinct, bijective with the 11 methods. Golden conformance vectors V-10..V-12 (P1a §9). | `docs/specs/unit-a1-crud-routing-proxy.md` §4/§5.1/§5.10 |
| **DECIDED: ENCODE+DECODE — the A1 client ENCODES the request envelope (method + args) AND DECODES the response envelope (result or error), vs Unit GN's decode-only** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A1** — spec §4) | The client ENCODES the request envelope (method + args) and DECODES the response envelope (result or error). The golden vectors V-10..V-12 (P1a §9) are the byte-exact conformance targets for both directions. The decoder reproduces P1a §10: a malformed CRUD response body → `EngineError` (502); a well-formed body failing a CRUD-specific invariant → `EngineError` (502) naming the `CrudValidationFailure` variant (DECODE-THEN-VALIDATE-FOR-CRUD, consumed). | `docs/specs/unit-a1-crud-routing-proxy.md` §4/§5.2/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-RETRY — a bounded, opt-in retry-on-`EngineUnavailable` for the two mutating create methods (`createDocument`, `createWiki`); idempotency-key-based dedup is DEFERRED to A2** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A1** — spec §4/§5.10) | A1 delivers a **bounded, opt-in retry-on-`EngineUnavailable`** for the two mutating create methods (`createDocument`, `createWiki`), configured via the factory `retry` option (`maxRetries` default 0 = off, `backoffMs` default 100). The retry is **only** on `EngineUnavailable` (503) — the D2 engine-absent/transient transport case — and is **bounded** (never infinite). The retry re-issues the same request envelope after a fresh READY gate. **Idempotency-key-based dedup is explicitly DEFERRED to A2** (recorded decision, §5.10): P1a's frozen wire carries **no idempotency-key field**, and A1 must not invent wire fields; the A2 unit owns the caller-side dedup/UX for the duplicate-create risk. | `docs/specs/unit-a1-crud-routing-proxy.md` §4/§5.1/§5.10 |
| **DECIDED: A1-LIST-SUMMARY-DECODE — the A1 `listDocuments` contract pins `DocumentList.items: DocumentSummary[]` (NOT `Document[]`), decoded by the lenient `decodeDocumentSummary` (six fields, snake+camel; a full `Document` item is projected; only a missing/typed-wrong summary field or a non-object item → `EngineError('malformed document')` 502)** | 2026-09-11 (**LANDED + LIVE-VERIFIED**, Unit A1 re-derivation — closes `HOST-CRUD-LIST-SUMMARY-DECODE`) | The Gnosis engine's `listDocuments` (Gnosis §4.1.3, `src/store/mod.rs`) returns a lightweight `DocumentSummary` per item — six fields only, NO `graph`/`tags`/`created_at`/`author` — so the pre-fix host contract `DocumentList.items: Document[]` was **over-strong** and ALWAYS threw `malformed document` (502) on a non-empty wiki (a full `Document` is never on the list wire; the engine is correct, the host over-required). Re-derived: (1) a host `DocumentSummary` interface mirrors the wire (`documentId`/`wikiId`/`title`/`state`/`revision`/`updatedAt`, snake→camel); (2) `DocumentList.items: DocumentSummary[]`; (3) `decodeDocumentList` maps each item through the NEW lenient `decodeDocumentSummary` (never requires `graph`/`tags`/`created_at`/`author`; a full `Document` item is valid and projected to its six-field summary); (4) a genuinely malformed summary (missing/typed-wrong `document_id` etc.) or a non-object item still throws `EngineError('malformed document')` (502), matching the strict `decodeDocument` throw message. Verified: 8 new summary-decode regression tests in `tests/unit-a1-crud-routing-proxy.test.ts` (`§5.2 core-red`, `§5.1 shape round-trip`, `§5.3 re-derived`, `§5.8-9 golden summary`, `§5.9-14`, `HOST-1`, `HOST-2`) + the `P-IM-4` negative-generator tightening (all six fields, snake_case AND camelCase, incl. `wiki_id`/`updated_at`, + the non-object items) + the full-`Document`-item projection. The list-decode fail-state contract is pinned in A1 §5.2/§5.3/§5.9-14. Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the fresh relaunch: `gnosis.document.list` resolves to the typed `DocumentList` on populated (`items` six-field `DocumentSummary[]`, `total:1`) and empty (`items:[]`) wikis — no `malformed document`, no `-32602`. | `docs/specs/unit-a1-crud-routing-proxy.md` §3 (FINDING)/§5.1/§5.2/§5.3/§5.9-14 +  `archive/greens/2026-09-21-unit-a1-crud-list-summary-decode-greens.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor: the owning unit spec + the DONE row carrying the verification; archived basename unit-a1-crud-list-summary-decode-greens.md) |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-CRUD-EDIT-GROUP — a NEW `ToolGroup` union member `'gnosis-edit'` (mutating, default-off) is added to `security.ts`, DISTINCT from the read-only `gnosis` group; the 7 mutating document/wiki tools sit in `gnosis-edit`, the 4 read-only in `gnosis`; editing is NEVER a `code`-group op** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A2** — spec §5.2) | The `ToolGroup` union (line 3) becomes `'read'|'dispatch'|'graph'|'code'|'module'|'rag'|'edit'|'gnosis'|'gnosis-edit'`; 11 `TOOL_GROUPS` rows (4 read-only → `'gnosis'`, 7 mutating → `'gnosis-edit'`); `'gnosis-edit'` added to `VALID_GROUPS` (line 185). `defaultSecurityConfig()` is UNCHANGED (the default enabled set stays `['read','dispatch']` — `gnosis-edit` is OFF by default, a human enables it via the manual-UI settings pane). The 11 names are added to `ALL_TOOLS` (`mcp-server.ts:1735-1745`). A `gnosis.*` name NOT in `TOOL_GROUPS` → `null` (fail-closed). The `gnosis-edit` group is NOT a renderer seam (no `RpcMethod`/`MUTATING_METHODS` members — deliberately absent; the five-seam-gate reconciliation). | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.2 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-DOCUMENT-TOOLS — the 11 `gnosis.document.*`/`gnosis.wiki.*` MCP tools (create/get/update/delete/publish/unpublish/archive/list documents; create/get/list wikis), main-process-routed through the SAME `handleGnosisTool` handler as the retrieval trio** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A2** — spec §5.1/§5.3) | The 11 tools are registered in the `graph` array of `mcp-server.ts` `registerTools` and routed to the SAME main-process `handleGnosisTool` handler (never routed to the renderer). The tool-name → CRUD-method mapping is a bijection (the §5.7 `P-IM-1` mapping-family bijection — merged from the retired `P-TP-1` at the H-6 re-derivation). The body-construction rule pins the `null`-when-`None` discipline (P1a §4.2 — `null` for absent `Option` fields, NEVER `[]`). The read-only tools carry NO `caller`; the mutating tools thread the caller's edit-authority credential (H3). NONE of the 11 tools' `inputSchema` accepts a credential arg (A7 — no `token`/`tls`/`ca`/`cert`/`key`/`apiKey`; the `callerId` is an identity, NOT a credential). The CRUD tools do NOT record to the `QueryAuditLog` (the security surface is the group gate + the RBAC caller check). | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.1/§5.3 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-RBAC-CALLER-STORE — a NEW shell-side `AuthorityStore` maps a caller identity (a human/agent user) to their edit-authority credential (the opaque `caller` string the engine's RBAC check consumes); the mutating tools present the caller's authority credential on each mutating call; a caller with no edit authority is denied caller-side** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A2** — spec §5.4) | The `AuthorityStore` (`src/main/authority-store.ts` — `createAuthorityStore(loadAuthorityMapping())`, `callerCredential`/`editors()`) resolves the caller's edit-authority credential; the handler threads it into the mutating args' `caller` field (the engine is the RBAC enforcer — A2 wires the store to the check, it does NOT re-implement enforcement). A `callerId` that resolves to no credential (or a null/absent `authorityStore`) is denied caller-side (`Error('<tool>: caller has no edit authority')`, no proxy call issued — fail-closed). | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.4 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-409-UX — the optimistic-concurrency 409 UX (H2) surfaces `ConflictError` (409) on BOTH the MCP tools and the GUI document-editor screens** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A2** — spec §5.5/§5.6) | A stale `baseRevision` on `gnosis.document.update` → `ConflictError` (409) propagates as the MCP tool error; the `gnosis-documents` GUI pane renders the conflict state (the conflict message) on a 409, never a crash. The pane re-reads the document (`getDocument`) on a 409 to obtain the current revision, or shows the conflict message + a re-read prompt. | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.5/§5.6 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-IDEMPOTENCY-DEDUP — a NEW caller-side `IdempotencyRegistry` dedups the duplicate-create risk (P4); P1a's frozen wire carries NO idempotency-key field, so the dedup is caller-side: the mutating create tools accept an optional `requestId`; a duplicate `requestId` returns the first result** | 2026-09-10 (**LANDED, Unit A2** — spec §5.4) | The `IdempotencyRegistry` (`src/main/idempotency-registry.ts` — `createIdempotencyRegistry({ maxEntries: 100 })`, LRU-bounded, never unbounded) caches the mutating create results under the `(callerId, requestId)` pair; a duplicate `(callerId, requestId)` returns the cached result WITHOUT issuing a new proxy call; a caller can NEVER receive another caller's cached result (caller-scoped). The GUI provides a fresh `requestId` per create action (the dedup coverage is the MCP retry path). | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.4 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-DOCS-PANE-WIKI-SELECTOR-ALWAYS — the `gnosisDocumentsContent` documents-pane render contract (H-6 / `HOST-GUI-DOCS-PANE-DEADLOCK`): the wiki selector renders whenever `state.wikis != null`, INDEPENDENT of `state.documents`; the whole-pane `unavailable` is reserved for `state == null || state.wikis == null`; the conflict state is checked FIRST; a null/empty `documents` renders the empty doc-list state `data-gnosis-docstate="empty"` (NEVER the whole-pane unavailable while the wiki selector is available); the render is TOTAL (never a native TypeError over a malformed `documents`/`wikis`)** | 2026-09-11 (**LANDED + LIVE-VERIFIED**, Unit A2 scoped re-derivation — closes `HOST-GUI-DOCS-PANE-DEADLOCK`) | The `gnosisDocumentsContent` render helper (`src/renderer/pane-graph.ts`) pins the **wiki-selector-ALWAYS** rule: the clickable wiki `<li>` items (the ONLY path that triggers `gnosis.document.list`, which populates `documents`) render whenever `state.wikis` is non-null — INDEPENDENT of `state.documents` — breaking the chicken-and-egg deadlock that left the pane stuck in whole-pane `unavailable`. Guard order pinned (unchanged conflict-first, then): `state == null || state.wikis == null` → the whole-pane `data-gnosis-state='unavailable'` (engine-absent); otherwise (wikis present) the wiki selector renders ALWAYS and a null OR empty `documents` renders the Document section's empty doc-list state `data-gnosis-docstate="empty"` ("No documents — select a wiki") — NEVER the whole-pane `unavailable` while the wiki selector is available and NEVER a TypeError (a non-array `documents`/`.items`/`wikis` coerces to `[]`). `state.conflict != null` renders the whole-pane `conflict` state, checked FIRST (even with null `wikis`/`documents`). Pinned by the §5.7 `P-IM-4` (`strat:documents-pane-render-total`) register row + §5.8-20 / §5.9-41. Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the fresh relaunch: the pane renders the wiki selector (10 live wiki `<li>` items) + `data-gnosis-docstate="empty"`, no whole-pane `unavailable`. | `docs/specs/unit-a2-document-crud-wiring.md` §5.5/§5.7/§5.8-20/§5.9-41 |

| **DECIDED: ENGINE-TRANSPORT-SHARED-MODULE — a shared `src/main/engine-transport.ts` module exports the 5 extracted transport helpers + the new `createEngineFetch(auth)`; both proxies consume it** | 2026-09-10 (LANDED, Unit shell-integration) | A NEW shared module `src/main/engine-transport.ts` exports the **5 extracted transport helpers** (`isLoopbackHost`/`assertLoopback`/`transportError`/`fetchWithTimeout`/`headers`) + the **new `createEngineFetch(auth)`** (the TLS application). Both proxies (`engine-rag-store.ts` = Unit GN, `engine-crud-rag-store.ts` = Unit A1) consume it; their duplicated local helper copies are REMOVED. `assertLoopback` is now exported from HERE (no longer internal to `engine-rag-store.ts` — the ASSERT-LOOPBACK-INTERNAL row is UPDATED); the proxies still do NOT export it and loopback enforcement is still tested through the factory throw pattern. `isLoopbackHost` is the **superset** (the union of both proxies' implementations, incl. the hex `::ffff:7f00:1` form added to GN). The `opts.fetch` injectable seam STAYS the test seam (`opts.fetch ?? createEngineFetch(opts.auth)`). The engine-transport ↔ engine-rag-store import cycle is an intentional safe ESM cycle (the error model referenced only at call time) — must NOT be "fixed" by moving the error model or duplicating the helpers. §5.1. | `docs/specs/unit-shell-integration.md` §5.1 |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-TLS-UNDICI — the TLS `dispatcher` dependency is justified + scoped: `undici` is a shell-side runtime dep (the engine lib stays at zero new runtime deps); `createEngineFetch(auth)` applies `auth.tls` via the undici `dispatcher`** | 2026-09-10 (LANDED, Unit shell-integration) | The standard `fetch` API does not accept a custom CA/cert/key without an https agent; undici's `fetch` accepts a `dispatcher` (an `Agent` with `connect: { ca, cert, key }`) — the standard way to apply a custom CA/cert/key in Node. When `auth.tls` is set, `createEngineFetch(auth)` returns a wrapper calling `undiciFetch(input, { ...init, dispatcher })` where the `dispatcher` is an `Agent({ connect: { ca, cert, key } })` carrying ONLY the PRESENT fields (an absent field is omitted, never passed as `undefined` — incl. the `{ tls: {} }` empty-`connect` edge, adversarial H2). When `auth.tls` is NOT set, it returns `globalThis.fetch` (no dispatcher). The `undici` dependency is added to the **Astrographer shell's** dependencies (shell-side only); the Gnosis engine lib stays at zero new runtime deps. The TLS path is exercised only when `auth.tls` is set (GUI-only). §5.1. | `docs/specs/unit-shell-integration.md` §5.1 |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-SSE-FETCH-CLIENT — a fetch-based SSE client (a `createSseClient(auth)` factory) replaces `EventSource` as the default; it sends the `Authorization: Bearer` header on the stream, does NOT auto-reconnect, and preserves the H10 premature-close `EngineUnavailable` semantics; the `SseClient` interface is unchanged** | 2026-09-10 (LANDED, Unit shell-integration) | The fetch-based SSE client factory `createSseClient(auth)` replaces the EventSource-based `defaultSseClient` (an EventSource can't send a custom `Authorization` header and auto-reconnects, fighting the H10 no-reconnect contract). The client: opens EXACTLY ONE `fetch(url, { method:'GET', headers:{ ...(auth?.token ? { authorization: `Bearer ${auth.token}` } : {}) }, signal })` — the Bearer token is sent on the stream and NEVER in the URL; it sends NO `content-type` on a GET stream (adversarial H1); a dropped connection is TERMINAL (fires `onError`/`onClose` exactly once, never re-opens); a clean stream end → `onClose()`; a fetch rejection / non-2xx / malformed-frame / mid-stream error → `onError`; `close()` → `controller.abort()` with NO further callbacks (a consumer teardown is NOT an `onClose`). The `SseClient` interface (`subscribe(url, handlers): { close() }`) is UNCHANGED; the proxy's `ragStream` consumption (the READY gate, the H10 premature-close handling) is UNCHANGED. §5.2. | `docs/specs/unit-shell-integration.md` §5.2 |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-TRANSPORT-POLICY — the concrete bind/auth/TLS policy record: bind loopback-only (enforced at construction); auth Bearer-on-the-`Authorization`-header for all request/response + the SSE stream (never in the URL); TLS via the shared https-agent-backed fetch; the GUI-only carve-out** | 2026-09-10 (LANDED, Unit shell-integration, DECIDED/ACTIVE) | **Bind:** the engine's `baseUrl` MUST resolve to a loopback address — the `isLoopbackHost` superset (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, the IPv4-mapped IPv6 loopback forms incl. `::ffff:127.0.0.1`/`::ffff:127.x.y.z`/the hex `::ffff:7f00:1`, and `localhost` — no DNS at construction); `assertLoopback` throws at construction (`Error('<prefix>: baseUrl must be loopback')`) on a non-loopback/unparseable `baseUrl` (synchronous, no network I/O). **Auth:** `auth.token` is sent as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on ALL request/response calls (via `headers(auth)`) AND on the SSE stream (via `createSseClient`); the token is NEVER in the URL; the token is GUI-only at the shell, blocked from MCP access (the `gnosis.*` carve-out, consumed GNOSIS-SECURITY-CARVE-OUT — no `gnosis.*` `inputSchema` accepts a `token`/`tls`/`ca`/`cert`/`key`/`apiKey` credential arg). **TLS:** `auth.tls` (`{ca,cert,key}`) is applied via `createEngineFetch` (the undici dispatcher); the TLS options are GUI-only at the shell, blocked from MCP access. **`baseUrl` is NOT a credential** (env/CLI config — the `resolveEngineBaseUrl()` seam). §5.3. | `docs/specs/unit-shell-integration.md` §5.3 |
| **DECIDED: GNOSIS-LAUNCHER-TOGGLE — `scripts/start-app.sh --mode=gnosis` spawns the P2 `gnosis-server` binary alongside the app and points the shell at it (the shell remains a pure client; the launcher does the orchestration)** | 2026-09-11 (**LANDED, config/launcher-only** — NO source, NO JS contract change; **AMENDED 2026-09-21** by the `GN-3` ruling — see the appended clause at the head of this cell) | **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.2 after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable] AMENDMENT CLAUSE (2026-09-21 — the `GN-3` ruling).** *"The launcher (`scripts/start-app.sh`) owns the spawn."* (verbatim, 2026-09-21). **What it decides:** the launcher **detects-or-launches, health-polls and reports**; **the shell stays a pure client**; **no new exec capability enters `src/`** — a shell-side spawn would be a **new exec/argv security seam and its own gated proposal**. **What it supersedes / amends: nothing.** It **CONFIRMS** this row's own recorded contract ("the shell remains a pure client; the launcher does the orchestration") and the architecture's `§3.2 B.3` clause 2; the amendment this row owes is the **provenance/amendment clause making the auto-start the launcher's DEFAULT** (the launcher-side change is that amendment's own follow-up, with its own diff — the **spawn mechanism itself is unchanged** and the gate record changes no script). **What it does NOT decide:** the launcher's exact default (auto-start vs `--mode=gnosis` opt-in) and the health-poll **timeout/retry** policy — pinned as the program's **P1 launcher item**, not by the gate record. **`src/` gains nothing:** `src/main/embeddings.ts`'s `execFileSync('curl', …)` ollama reachability probe remains the **only** `node:child_process` use in `src/`. **The sibling `GN-4` ruling AMENDS this row** (the "the engine is optional" rationale is amended — the engine is now a precondition of opening a wiki) while the spawn mechanism and launcher ownership are **unchanged**. Source: `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.2 (with `§13.1 P1`/`§3.2 B.3`). | Adds a `--mode=gnosis` toggle (orthogonal to the local retrieval embedder — `--mode=lexical|vector` still select it; `--mode=gnosis` only flips the spawn flag and keeps the embedder default). It spawns `$GNOSIS_SERVER_BIN` (default `$ROOT/../Gnosis/target/debug/gnosis-server`) on `$GNOSIS_SERVER_PORT` (default 8080 = the shell's default `resolveEngineBaseUrl()` target), waits for `GET /engine/status`, exports `PROVIDENT_ENGINE_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:$GS_PORT`, launches electron in the foreground (NOT `exec`) so the server is killed after the app exits (propagating electron's exit code). Optional `GNOSIS_SERVER_OLLAMA_URL`/`GNOSIS_SERVER_OLLAMA_MODEL` forward to the server for `state:Ready`. NOTE: the app's `resolveEngineBaseUrl` reads its CONFIG SEAM first, so an operator-set `provident-engine-config.json` `engineBaseUrl` override still wins over this export — by design. No Gnosis-workspace change required (the server is standalone; readiness is pollable via `/engine/status`). This is the Option-B spawn mechanism; the live-scenario batteries remain valid (they need the running app MCP/UI surface exercised, which is a live-session item). | Implemented in `scripts/start-app.sh` per the Option-B decision; verified live in the launcher (guard paths + spawn→READY→cleanup) |

| **DECIDED: UI-CONFIG-CARRIER (C9) — UI settings + configuration (theme, pane layout/order/collapse/size, pane visibility, open tabs, editing mode) serialize through the existing `OperatorSettings` store as one versioned shape** | 2026-09-11 (**ACTIVE — Wave-0 decision**, resolves ui-overhaul §7 Q24/Q6; no code yet) | The serialized UI-config state extends `OperatorSettings` (`src/main/operator-settings-store.ts`) rather than the engine `SerializedRenderDoc` or a new `UIState` doc. Rationale: reuses the store's proven fail-soft `sanitize`/defaults discipline; keeps UI config operator-scoped (the operator-settings IPC is NOT group-gated and NOT MCP-visible, consistent with `OPERATOR-ISOLATED-GRAPHSCOPE`); avoids a fourth persistent store. Shape gains additive optional fields (theme tri-state `system`/`light`/`dark`; `layout` `{zones, panes}` per ui-overhaul §3; `openTabs`/`activeTab`; existing `enabledPanes` plus the existing `editingMode`/`topK`/`defaultDocumentId`). Pinned rules: a `version` field; unknown/missing fields fall back to defaults; a corrupt file never crashes boot (mirror `sanitize`); **credentials are NEVER serialized here** (security token / engine `auth.token`/TLS / API keys stay in their dedicated non-exported stores — `GNOSIS-SECURITY-CARVE-OUT`); the UI-config state must never be MCP-exportable while carrying a secret. The `layout`/`theme`/`tabs` slices are added by their respective units (U-SHELL-1/2/9, U-SHELL-8), each through this one carrier (do not invent per-unit stores). | `docs/specs/ui-overhaul.md` §3.2/§7 Q24/§8.1 (Wave 0) |

The alternative (a fresh SecurePanels container + swapping its mount) is rejected as heavier + adapter-recreate risk. | **DECIDED: IMPORT-NO-SYMLINK-FOLLOW (C17, U-IMPORT-1) — the fs directory→`.md` expansion and the selection resolution SKIP all symlinks (`lstat` ⇒ excluded, never dereferenced), whether file- or directory-links** | 2026-09-14 (**ACTIVE — Architect ratification of W-U-IMPORT-1-Q1**) | `expandImportDirectory`/`resolveImportSelection` never follow a symlink (the conservative Q17 "no symlink follow"). A dir with >512 matching files ⇒ `{ ok:false, reason:'cap-exceeded' }`; exactly 512 ⇒ OK (boundary). The fail-loud behavior is binding regardless of the number. | Architect ruling 2026-09-14, `docs/specs/unit-u-import-1-import-surface.md` §8 |

The renderer renders it via the existing broadcast/IPC plumbing (a status toast/pane is outside this unit's scope). | Architect ruling 2026-09-14, `docs/specs/unit-u-import-1-import-surface.md` §8 |

| **DECIDED: ENGINE-ABSENT-DEGRADED-CONTRACT — with no engine every local path works identically; engine-absent reads are typed-unavailable, never silent** | 2026-09-17 (identity clause **REVERSED 2026-09-21**; the "never silent" clause **SURVIVES**) | **REVERSED (in part) 2026-09-21 by `DECIDED: ENGINE-ABSENT-REFUSES-WITHOUT-AN-ENGINE`** (`docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.3 — the `GN-4` user ruling; **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from the gate record §11.3 + §14.1 after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]**). **The clause at issue is the IDENTITY clause** — *"with no engine every local path works IDENTICALLY"* is **no longer true** (with no engine, **no wiki opens**); the second clause — an engine-absent read is a **typed unavailable, never a silent no-op**, and the launcher must fail loud — **SURVIVES and binds** (it becomes the refusal's failure vocabulary: a warning that names the missing engine is a **typed** failure, never a silent empty stage). The reversal is **recorded as a reversal, not a clarification** (§11.3). **Consequences owed:** `docs/specs/astrographer-scope-realignment-review.md` §3.4 is **superseded in substance** (the contract's source of record — the reversal is recorded **against** it); defects `DEMO-ENGINE-START-GAP` + `GNOSIS-SIDEBAR-SEAM-MISSING` **owe a status change** (their fix shape presumes the local path stays usable); catalog `PRUNE-829` (§C.4 `keep` — **non-prunable by construction, re-homed/re-pointed by the ruling's own pass, never pruned**) and its §C.3 twin `PRUNE-361` are **affected rows, cited never edited**. With no engine, every local path must work IDENTICALLY: local store boot (`src/main/rag-store-runtime.ts:662`, `src/main/rag-store-default.ts:31`), import atomicity + the 512 cap, local `rag.query`, and the synchronous `RagStore` reads must be unaffected by engine absence. **No offload unit may make a local path DEPEND on engine presence**; an engine-absent read must surface a **typed unavailable** error — never a silent no-op (the `DEMO-ENGINE-START-GAP` / `GNOSIS-SIDEBAR-SEAM-MISSING` class) — and **the launcher must fail loud**. | the same proposal gate 2026-09-17 (`docs/specs/astrographer-scope-realignment-review.md` §3.4); defects `DEMO-ENGINE-START-GAP`, `GNOSIS-SIDEBAR-SEAM-MISSING` |

| **DECIDED: ADVISORY-REQUIREMENT-CATALOG — the catalog is ADVISORY metadata, the tracker is the SOLE status authority, every prune needs a user sign-off, and no deletion may cite the catalog as its authority** | 2026-09-21 | **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from `docs/specs/requirement-catalog.md` §6.3/§3.6/§3.9 and `archive/reviews/2026-09-21-requirement-catalog-doc-review.md` §4 after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]** **(1) ADVISORY status.** `docs/requirement-catalog.md` is **ADVISORY metadata**: its rows are derived from the trackers/specs, they **cite** them by pointer (`status_pointer` / `evidence_pointer`), and they carry **no status authority of their own**. **(2) The tracker is the SOLE status authority** — the catalog reads a status through its pointer and may never assert one; a conflict between the catalog and a tracker row is resolved by a **tracker/spec amendment, never by the catalog** (the C13/N1 conflict is the worked case). **(3) The prune criterion** — `escalate-prune` requires **all three** clauses: *`no requirement origin of ANY kind`* (no user origin, no decision origin, no pinned-spec origin, no upstream-contract origin) **AND** `no non-test consumer` **AND** `no live-confirmed regression pin`; because the first clause is absence-based and this repo demonstrably has unwritten/dangling user provenance (the report-#3 lineage), the criterion is **not sufficient alone**. **(4) POSITIVE NEGATIVE EVIDENCE (mandatory).** A row may reach `escalate-prune` only with an **`origin_search` record** in `verdict_basis` (date, the enumerated source set — the six tracker inputs + `docs/HANDOFF.md` + `docs/feature-requests/*` + the report #1/#2 docs + the report-#3 prose block + `docs/specs/**` — and the result), **plus** a documented-reviewer confirmation at the row's section pointer, **plus** survival of **one full review cycle at `escalate-prune`** (a one-cycle cooldown — a candidate may never be deleted in the cycle it is raised). **(5) THE SIGN-OFF GATE.** `prune_signoff: user-approved:<date>` is **MANDATORY before any deletion**; `prune_signoff: none` makes a row **non-deletable by construction**, and **no deletion may cite the catalog as its authority** — the owning tracker row must carry it. **Freezes:** any row whose provenance is `user-verbatim` / `user-directed-record` / `user-ratified-spec`, **or** whose lineage touches a **dangling citation**, **freezes at `keep-advisory`** and can never escalate; `stale` / `invalidated-pointer` / `invalidated-conflict` rows may **NEVER** be pruned; §C.4's upstream-owed rows are **non-prunable by construction** (cited, never edited). **(6) THE SHRINK/TOMBSTONE RULE (deferred; recorded so the generator unit inherits it).** When a prune actually lands (a future unit, with user sign-off): the register row is **deleted in the same pass**, **appended verbatim** to `archive/catalog/<date>-pruned.md`, and — **because `archive/` is gitignored** — the same pass MUST leave a **git-visible tombstone**: a `PRUNE-###` line in the **deletion commit message** naming every id **and** a **tombstone row** in the catalog (`PRUNE id, date, sign-off, landing commit, deleted paths/spans`). **The archive copy is NOT evidence; the tombstone is.** A retired `PRUNE-###` id is never reused. A `merged-into:<id>` row **keeps its register slot** (not deleted). **(7) The item-10d UPDATE TRIGGER.** The catalog's §C.8 change log opens a new cycle whenever a **tracker-editing pass** lands (a cycle = **one landing pass of a tracker-editing unit**); a tracker edit that does not open the cycle is a drift. **(8) THE MINIMAL `D-GP-UFA` AMENDMENT (C6).** The checklist's three new columns are **METADATA**: they do **not** touch the `§5.U` ≤8-row cap, they do **not** touch `MATRIX_ROWS`, and they do **not** touch the §6.1 report schema; the row count is **DERIVED** and **zero new matrix rows** are claimed. **(9) This unit deletes NOTHING** — not a register row, not a tracker row, not a file, not a cell. **Layer: DOC-LAYER/ADVISORY — no app/envelope/store/live claim** (RCA-12). | `docs/specs/requirement-catalog.md` §6.3 + §3.6 + §3.5/§3.9 + §3.7; `docs/requirement-catalog.md` §C.0/§C.3/§C.4/§C.8; item-10d review `archive/reviews/2026-09-21-requirement-catalog-doc-review.md` §4 |

| **DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD — the engine owns document CRUD; Astrographer does not persist documents (the `GN-1` ruling)** | 2026-09-21 | **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.1/§11.5/§12.7/§13/§14.1/§14.2/§14.5 after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]** *(Verbatim ruling: "Supersede now — engine owns document CRUD." (2026-09-21).)* **(1) THE DECISION.** The **engine becomes the authority for document CRUD**; **Astrographer does not persist documents**. The rows superseded by this ruling are `DECIDED: RAG-AUTHORITATIVE`, `DECIDED: SINGLE-WRITER-STORE` and `DECIDED: SINGLE-WRITER-STORE-PER-STORE`, all three **marked SUPERSEDED in place** and enumerated in the file's SUPERSEDED table below. **(2) `DECIDED: ASTROGRAPHER-SCOPE-REALIGNMENT` is AMENDED, NOT superseded in full** — its presentation-layer half stays ACTIVE and binding; its ownership clause is reversed (see that row's appended clause). **(3) THE HOST KEEPS NO DURABLE CORPUS** of documents: the local store is a **TEMPORARY authority with a recorded sunset condition** until P2 lands — the sunset fires when **`U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH` (O-8)** lands and the authority-switch row records the cutover; from that point **any surviving local document write is a fence violation**, not a temporary authority, and **no pass may describe the cutover as already done**. **(4) THE TAB-SCOPED READ CACHE CLAUSE (the read model, verbatim: "Host read cache exists only for currently tab-owned documents and pane data. Opening a new tab requires an async call to Gnosis to load the data into cache.").** The **tab-scoped-cache pivot** is SELECTED (`docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.5/§12): **residency is a precondition of rendering**; a **cache miss is a defined failure, never a silent empty result**; the **tab-open fetch is the async seam**; eviction and the dirty-tab case are pinned; and the **sync-read pin stands for cache-resident data only** (its subject becomes the cache, never the store). `GN-1`'s "persists" means **tabs' own state, not document data**. **(5) MCP + the security/operator stores STAY HOST-OWNED.** `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` is host-side by contract, and `GNOSIS-SECURITY-CARVE-OUT`, `ENGINE-TRANSPORT-POLICY`, `IPC-SURFACE-NOT-GROUP-GATED`, `OPERATOR-ISOLATED-GRAPHSCOPE` plus the host-side stores (`security-store`, `authority-store`, `idempotency-registry`, `query-audit`, `OperatorSettings`, the vector cache) and `docs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md` are **NOT superseded and NOT prunable** by this ruling — "Astrographer persists no documents" is read as **documents**, never as these stores. **(6) The multi-store registry STAYS ACTIVE:** `DECIDED: MULTI-STORE-REGISTRY` + `DECIDED: FANOUT-INTERLEAVE-MERGE` (catalog `PRUNE-835`) remain ACTIVE and not prunable. **(7) THE FENCE IS EXEMPT AND RE-PLANNED, NOT RE-DERIVED:** `tests/import-render-no-duplicates.test.ts` + `tests/traversal.test.ts` remain **exempt by name** and may not be re-derived by any `GN-*` unit; the fence plan is §14.2 and **lands WITH `U-READS-PIVOT`** (verification is a P2 gate — the fence is **run** as a regression reading, never asserted green from a plan). A unit that cannot stay green under the fence is **not** a `GN-1` implementation — it is a new proposal re-entering the gate. **(8) THE ASYNC WRITE-PATH REVERSAL.** The commit path becomes an **async engine write**, **reversing** `docs/specs/astrographer-scope-realignment-review.md` §3.3's WRITE PATH row ("commit-on-blur editing cannot be async-chunked behind an engine hop"); the unit that must **restate the commit contract** is the `WHOLE-PAGE-EDITING` unit (`C9` `U-EDIT-1`) — no unit implements an async commit before that restatement. `RAG-EDIT-MCP-GROUPS` (host `edit`) and `GNOSIS-CRUD-EDIT-GROUP` (engine `gnosis-edit`) **both stay ACTIVE**; `edit.set_content` becomes an **alias at the switch**, via an amendment to `RAG-EDIT-MCP-GROUPS` + `MCP-UI-EQUIVALENCE` — **explicitly not implied by this ruling**. **(9) THE TEMPORARY-AUTHORITY GAP + THE CORPUS-LOSS CONDITION (recorded, never hidden; the `K2` risk materialised).** The contract **says** engine-authoritative while the code **still writes locally**; the engine **persists NOTHING today** (O-7 unmet), so **without `U-ENGINE-PERSIST` + `U-CORPUS-MIGRATION` the operator corpus — 226 documents / 6 102 nodes / 9 266 edges — is LOST at the next restart.** **The fence:** **nothing cuts over until those units land**, and the gap is **not a licence** (it authorises no new local-authoritative feature and lowers no P2 gate). **(10) `DECIDED: SOURCE-SWITCHABLE`'s anticipated `createRemoteRagStore`** is **discharged/cited, not superseded** — the ruling makes that clause **current** rather than anticipated. **What this ruling does NOT decide (each owed, each with a named owner):** the migration story (`U-CORPUS-MIGRATION`, owed **before any cutover**, with journal/undo + rollback); the security/operator seam and the MCP contract (unchanged); the **engine-absent** behaviour (the sibling `DECIDED: ENGINE-ABSENT-REFUSES-WITHOUT-AN-ENGINE`); and whether the refusal is **boot-wide or per-wiki** (`U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH`'s spec). **Layer: DOC-LAYER / contract change — nothing here is app-green** (RCA-12). | user ruling 2026-09-21 (verbatim) → `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.1/§11.5/§12/§13/§14.1/§14.2/§14.5; upstream rows in `docs/HANDOFF.md` §OPEN handoff items (`O-7 ENGINE PERSISTENCE`, `O-8 AUTHORITY SWITCH / OFFLINE DUAL-PATH`, GR-5, the GR inventory, the durability direction) |
| **DECIDED: ENGINE-ABSENT-REFUSES-WITHOUT-AN-ENGINE — with no Gnosis instance the app warns and does not open a wiki (the `GN-4` reversal)** | 2026-09-21 | **[RECONSTRUCTED 2026-09-21 — re-derived from `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.3/§12.5/§14.1 after the tracker-loss incident; verbatim wording of the destroyed row is not recoverable]** *(Verbatim ruling: "Refuse to open a wiki without an engine. The app warns and does not open a wiki when no Gnosis instance can be found." (2026-09-21).)* **What it decides:** when **no Gnosis instance can be found**, the app **warns the user and does not open a wiki**. **This REVERSES `DECIDED: ENGINE-ABSENT-DEGRADED-CONTRACT`** — the reversed clause is its **identity clause** ("with no engine every local path works IDENTICALLY"), recorded **as a reversal, not a clarification**. **It AMENDS `DECIDED: GNOSIS-LAUNCHER-TOGGLE`** (an amendment, **not** a supersession): that row's toggle exists "precisely because the engine is optional", and the engine is now a **precondition of opening a wiki** — while the launcher's **spawn ownership is confirmed and its recorded contract intact**. **The typed-unavailable surface is RETAINED as the failure vocabulary** even though the identity contract is reversed: a warning that names the missing engine is a **typed** failure, never a silent empty stage. **Consequences, stated not softened:** a user without the engine binary can open **NO** wiki (the local store still exists on disk, written by nothing, and is **unreachable through the app's document surfaces**); **this row and `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD` interlock** — together they mean **engine absence is a total document-surface outage**, which is why `U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH` (O-8, the offline dual-path) is a **PREREQUISITE** and not a tail unit, and why the warning UX is a **first-class state** (the `TAB-1` class, §12.5) rather than an error page. **Affected rows (cited, never edited):** catalog `PRUNE-829` (§C.4 `keep` — **non-prunable by construction**; to be **re-homed/re-pointed by the ruling's own pass**) + its §C.3 twin `PRUNE-361`; `docs/specs/astrographer-scope-realignment-review.md` §3.4 is **superseded in substance**; defects `DEMO-ENGINE-START-GAP` + `GNOSIS-SIDEBAR-SEAM-MISSING` **owe a status change**; the `docs/HANDOFF.md` **O-8 pointer row** records the consequence (offline-write reconciliation becomes a **hard** requirement). **What it does NOT decide:** whether the refusal is **boot-wide or per-wiki** (the read model pins the **per-wiki/tab-open** surfacing as the load-bearing case and leaves the boot-wide framing to `U-AUTHORITY-SWITCH`'s spec); whether **operator surfaces outside documents** stay usable (settings, security/operator panes, template data are `UI-CONFIG-CARRIER` state, **not** documents); and the **warning's exact wording/affordance** (toast / modal / inline stage state — a spec obligation on the implementing unit). **Layer: DOC-LAYER / contract change — nothing here is app-green** (RCA-12). | user ruling 2026-09-21 (verbatim) → `docs/specs/design-extensions-review.md` §11.3 (with §12.5/§13 P2/§14.1 C-8); `docs/HANDOFF.md` (the O-8 pointer row) |

| **DECIDED: DOCUMENT-CONSISTENCY-IS-A-PRODUCT-FEATURE — document-consistency tracking is an intended FEATURE of Gnosis/Astrographer (engine-side, the same direction as `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`); the repo-side requirement-catalog generator unit is therefore REDUNDANT and is PARKED, not deleted; the ADVISORY catalog is NOT retired by this row** | 2026-09-21 | **(1) THE FEATURE.** **Document-consistency tracking — detecting and surfacing documents or statements that have gone stale or contradict one another — IS AN INTENDED FEATURE of Gnosis/Astrographer**, owned **engine-side**, in the **same direction as `DECIDED: ENGINE-AUTHORITATIVE-DOCUMENT-CRUD`**: the **engine owns the corpus** and derives/detects over it, and the **host presents** what the engine reports. It is **not** a repo tooling duty and **not** a host-side indexer. **(2) THE REDUNDANCY.** Because the feature lives engine-side, the **repo-side derivation tooling is REDUNDANT**: `scripts/catalog-derive.mjs` + `tests/requirement-catalog-contract.test.ts` (spec `docs/specs/unit-catalog-generator.md`) — the unit that would have mechanized a **project-local** consistency/traceability index over the docs. **The repo MUST NOT build a second, project-local consistency indexer**: a project-local second mechanism would be a second authority over the same question and the same class of divergence the engine's feature exists to answer. **(3) PARKED, NOT DELETED.** The unit is **PARKED**: `docs/specs/unit-catalog-generator.md` is **RETAINED as the fallback record** (its contract, its carried clause set (a)–(f) and its register are **not** withdrawn and **nothing in it is scheduled**), and its status block carries a dated `PARKED 2026-09-21` line. **THE REVISIT CONDITION (the named trigger — either clause ends the park):** (a) **the product feature does NOT cover the repo's own requirement docs** (i.e. it does not detect stale/contradictory **in-repo tracker/spec prose**, which is the corpus the generator derived over), **or** (b) **the catalog's bookkeeping drifts measurably again** (a count/waiver/pointer divergence that a hand pass misses — the class that has already cost the catalog two fix cycles, `docs/requirement-catalog.md` §C.8). **(4) THE DISTINCTION — the ADVISORY catalog is NOT retired by this row.** What (2) parks is **the repo-side DERIVATION TOOLING**; the **ADVISORY catalog itself** (`docs/requirement-catalog.md`) is a **different object** — it **REMAINS the repo-side pointer index**, **advisory and non-authoritative**, and **every rule of the `DECIDED: ADVISORY-REQUIREMENT-CATALOG` row still holds** (the tracker is the SOLE status authority; no prune without a user sign-off; no deletion may cite the catalog as its authority). **The two objects must not be confused:** the catalog is **a set of advisory rows that cite tracker state**, the generator is **a tool that would have recomputed some of their cells**. **If the owner intends the CATALOG to be retired as well, that is a SEPARATE ruling, and THIS row does not make it.** **(5) THE BOOKKEEPING IS NOW HAND-OWED (nothing is silently dropped).** The duties the generator would have mechanized are now **hand-owed**, re-verified by the **item-10d documentation review** until the park ends (`docs/specs/requirement-catalog.md` §4.4;
